C.34
),

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\subseteq[m]\\
|S|\geq 2\end{subarray}}\prod_{j\in S}\varepsilon_{j}\;\leq\;2^{m-2}\sum_{a<b}\varepsilon_{a}\varepsilon_{b}\;\leq\;2^{m-2}(8C_{1})^{2}\,\rho^{2}\!\sum_{a<b}\!\sqrt{\frac{\mu r_{a}}{d_{a}}}\sqrt{\frac{\mu r_{b}}{d_{b}}}\;\leq\;C(\mu,r,m)\,\rho^{2}.

(C.37)

Core tensor factor.

Since
\hat{T}=\hat{C}\times_{1}\hat{U}_{1,0}\cdots\times_{m}\hat{U}_{m,0}
and
\hat{T}\approx T^{\star}
,

\frac{\left\lVert\hat{C}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}}{\sqrt{d^{\star}}}\;\leq\;\frac{\left\lVert\hat{T}\right\rVert_{F}}{\sqrt{d^{\star}}}\;\leq\;\left\lVert\hat{T}\right\rVert_{\infty}\;\leq\;\left\lVert T^{\star}\right\rVert_{\infty}+o(1)\;=\;O(1),

which is bounded by a constant depending only on the model parameters (bounded entries in the BTL model).

Combining.

Substituting (
C.36
) and (
C.37
) into Proposition
C.13
:

\left\lVert(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}\;\leq\;\left\lVert\hat{C}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\cdot 2^{m}\sqrt{\frac{\mu^{m}r^{\star}}{d^{\star}}}\cdot C(\mu,r,m)\,\rho^{2}\;=\;C(\mu,r,m)\cdot\frac{\left\lVert\hat{C}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}}{\sqrt{d^{\star}}}\cdot\rho^{2}\;\leq\;C(\mu,r,m)\,\rho^{2}.

By (
C.32
),

\boxed{|R_{\mathrm{proj}}|\;\leq\;2^{m}\,C(\mu,r,m)\;\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\;\rho^{2}.}

(C.38)

C.7
H-Direction Bias

This term is the population-level error from using
\hat{H}
instead of
H^{\star}
. After a Taylor expansion around
\eta^{\star}
, the first-order piece involves
\langle G\Delta,\hat{H}-H^{\star}\rangle
, which is controlled by the off-tangent components of
\Delta
(since the tangent-space component cancels via the first-order cancellation mechanism in Section
C.8
). The second-order piece is bounded by
C_{A}\|\Delta\|_{\infty}^{2}\|\Gamma\|_{1}
using the
\ell_{1}
-average bound, where the key cancellation is
d^{\star}
from
\|A^{-1}\|_{1\to 1}
against
1/d^{\star}
from the comparison averaging.

The H-direction bias term is

\mathbb{P}^{\star}\big[S_{\hat{T}}(\hat{H})-S_{\hat{T}}(H^{\star})\big]=\mathbb{P}^{\star}\big[s_{\eta}(Y,\hat{\eta})\left\langle\hat{H}-H^{\star},X\right\rangle\big].

C.7.1
Taylor expansion and leading term

Expanding
s_{\eta}(Y,\hat{\eta})
around
\eta^{\star}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\star}\big[s_{\eta}(Y,\hat{\eta})\left\langle\hat{H}-H^{\star},X\right\rangle\big]

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\mathbb{P}^{\star}\big[s_{\eta}(Y,\eta^{\star})\left\langle\hat{H}-H^{\star},X\right\rangle\big]}_{=\,0\text{ (score mean-zero)}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\mathbb{P}^{\star}\big[\dot{s}_{\eta}(Y,\eta^{\star})\left\langle\Delta,X\right\rangle\left\langle\hat{H}-H^{\star},X\right\rangle\big]}_{\text{first-order in }\Delta}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{P}^{\star}\big[\ddot{s}_{\eta}(Y,\bar{\eta})\left\langle\Delta,X\right\rangle^{2}\left\langle\hat{H}-H^{\star},X\right\rangle\big]}_{\text{second-order in }\Delta}.

(C.39)

C.7.2
\ell_{1}
-average bound under comparison design

Lemma C.14
(
\ell_{1}
-average bound).

Under the comparison design, for any tensor
v\in\mathcal{H}
,

\mathbb{E}^{\star}\big[|\left\langle v,X\right\rangle|\big]\leq\frac{\left\lVert v\right\rVert_{1}}{d^{\star}}.

(C.40)

Proof of Lemma
C.14
.

Since
X=e_{u,a}-e_{u,b}
with
(u,\{a,b\})
uniform,

|\left\langle v,X\right\rangle|=|v_{u,a}-v_{u,b}|\leq|v_{u,a}|+|v_{u,b}|
.
Taking expectation,

\mathbb{E}^{\star}[|\left\langle v,X\right\rangle|]\leq\frac{1}{D\binom{d_{1}}{2}}\sum_{u}\sum_{a<b}(|v_{u,a}|+|v_{u,b}|)=\frac{1}{D\binom{d_{1}}{2}}\,(d_{1}-1)\sum_{u}\sum_{a}|v_{u,a}|=\frac{2}{Dd_{1}}\,\left\lVert v\right\rVert_{1}.

Since
d^{\star}=D(d_{1}-1)/2
and
d_{1}/(d_{1}-1)\geq 1
,

\frac{2}{Dd_{1}}=\frac{d_{1}-1}{d_{1}}\cdot\frac{1}{d^{\star}}\leq\frac{1}{d^{\star}}
.

\square

C.7.3
Dimension-free
\left\lVert P_{\mathbb{T}}E_{\omega}\right\rVert_{1}
bound

Lemma C.15
(Dimension-free
\ell_{1}
bound for tangent projection).

Under
\mu
-incoherence, for any canonical basis tensor
E_{\omega}=e_{i_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes e_{i_{m}}
,

\left\lVert P_{\mathbb{T}}E_{\omega}\right\rVert_{1}\leq C_{P},\qquad C_{P}:=C(\mu,r,m).

(C.41)

The same bound holds for
\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}
under the incoherence of the estimated subspaces

\left\lVert e_{a}^{\top}\hat{U}_{j,0}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}\leq 2\mu r_{j}/d_{j}
.

Proof of Lemma
C.15
.

For elementary tensors, the Kronecker
\ell_{1}
factorization gives
\left\lVert a^{(1)}\otimes\cdots\otimes a^{(m)}\right\rVert_{1}=\prod_{j}\left\lVert a^{(j)}\right\rVert_{1}
.
By Cauchy–Schwarz and incoherence, each single-mode factor satisfies

\left\lVert P_{j}e_{i_{j}}\right\rVert_{1}=\sum_{k}|(P_{j})_{k,i_{j}}|\leq\sqrt{d_{j}}\,\left\lVert P_{j}e_{i_{j}}\right\rVert_{2}\leq\sqrt{d_{j}}\sqrt{\mu r_{j}/d_{j}}=\sqrt{\mu r_{j}},

and
\left\lVert P_{j}^{\perp}e_{i_{j}}\right\rVert_{1}\leq\left\lVert e_{i_{j}}\right\rVert_{1}+\left\lVert P_{j}e_{i_{j}}\right\rVert_{1}\leq 1+\sqrt{\mu r_{j}}
.
Using
P_{\mathbb{T}}=\Pi_{\emptyset}+\sum_{k}\Pi_{\{k\}}
and the Kronecker factorization,

\left\lVert\Pi_{\emptyset}(E_{\omega})\right\rVert_{1}=\prod_{j}\left\lVert P_{j}e_{i_{j}}\right\rVert_{1}\leq\mu^{m/2}\sqrt{r^{\star}}
,
and

\left\lVert\Pi_{\{k\}}(E_{\omega})\right\rVert_{1}=\left\lVert P_{k}^{\perp}e_{i_{k}}\right\rVert_{1}\prod_{j\neq k}\left\lVert P_{j}e_{i_{j}}\right\rVert_{1}\leq(1+\sqrt{\mu r_{k}})\prod_{j\neq k}\sqrt{\mu r_{j}}
.
Summing over blocks yields
\left\lVert P_{\mathbb{T}}E_{\omega}\right\rVert_{1}\leq C(\mu,r,m)
, which is dimension-free.
The bound for
\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}
follows identically with
2\mu
replacing
\mu
.

\square

C.7.4
Bounding the first-order piece

Proposition C.16
(H-direction bias bound).

Under Assumption
C.5
, the first-order piece satisfies

\big|\left\langle G\Delta,\hat{H}-H^{\star}\right\rangle\big|\leq C(\mu,r,m)\,C_{A}\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\Big[\left\lVert(I-\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}+\left\lVert(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}\Big].

(C.42)

Proof of Proposition
C.16
.

By (
C.9
),

\mathbb{P}^{\star}[\dot{s}_{\eta}(Y,\eta^{\star})\left\langle\Delta,X\right\rangle\left\langle\hat{H}-H^{\star},X\right\rangle]=\left\langle G\Delta,\hat{H}-H^{\star}\right\rangle.

Since
G
is symmetric and
\hat{A}^{-1}\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}
,
A^{-1}P_{\mathbb{T}}
are self-adjoint,

\left\langle G\Delta,\hat{H}-H^{\star}\right\rangle=\left\langle(\hat{A}^{-1}\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}G-A^{-1}P_{\mathbb{T}}G)\Delta,\Gamma\right\rangle.

By Hölder’s inequality,
|\left\langle u,\Gamma\right\rangle|\leq\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\cdot\left\lVert u\right\rVert_{\infty}
.
Decomposing via the triangle inequality,

\left\lVert(\hat{A}^{-1}\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}G-A^{-1}P_{\mathbb{T}}G)\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\left\lVert\hat{A}^{-1}\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}G(I-\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}+\left\lVert A^{-1}P_{\mathbb{T}}G(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}+\left\lVert(\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}.

Using

\left\lVert(\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\left\lVert(I-\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}+\left\lVert(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}

and Remark
C.6
yields (
C.42
).

\square

C.7.5
The second-order piece

By
|\ddot{s}_{\eta}|\leq c_{2}/\sigma^{2}
and
|\left\langle\Delta,X\right\rangle|\leq 2\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}
,

\left|\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{P}^{\star}\big[\ddot{s}_{\eta}\left\langle\Delta,X\right\rangle^{2}\left\langle\hat{H}-H^{\star},X\right\rangle\big]\right|\leq\frac{2c_{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\,\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}\,\mathbb{E}^{\star}[|\left\langle\hat{H}-H^{\star},X\right\rangle|].

We now bound
\mathbb{E}^{\star}[|\left\langle\hat{H}-H^{\star},X\right\rangle|]
using the
\ell_{1}
-average
(Lemma
C.14
):

\mathbb{E}^{\star}\big[|\left\langle\hat{H}-H^{\star},X\right\rangle|\big]\leq\frac{\left\lVert\hat{H}-H^{\star}\right\rVert_{1}}{d^{\star}}.

(C.43)

Step 1:
\ell_{1}
extraction.

By the
\ell_{1}
-version of Lemma
C.33
,

\left\lVert\hat{H}-H^{\star}\right\rVert_{1}=\left\lVert(\hat{A}^{-1}\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}-A^{-1}P_{\mathbb{T}})\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\leq\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\,\max_{\omega}\,\left\lVert(\hat{A}^{-1}\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}-A^{-1}P_{\mathbb{T}})E_{\omega}\right\rVert_{1}.

(C.44)

Step 2: triangle inequality and symmetry.

For each basis tensor
E_{\omega}
,

\left\lVert(\hat{A}^{-1}\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}-A^{-1}P_{\mathbb{T}})E_{\omega}\right\rVert_{1}\leq\left\lVert\hat{A}^{-1}\right\rVert_{1\to 1}\,\left\lVert\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}E_{\omega}\right\rVert_{1}+\left\lVert A^{-1}\right\rVert_{1\to 1}\,\left\lVert P_{\mathbb{T}}E_{\omega}\right\rVert_{1}.

Since
\hat{A}^{-1}
and
A^{-1}
are symmetric on their respective tangent spaces,
Lemma
C.38
gives

\left\lVert\hat{A}^{-1}\right\rVert_{1\to 1}=\left\lVert\hat{A}^{-1}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\leq C_{A}\,\sigma^{2}d^{\star}

(Assumption
C.5
), and likewise for
A^{-1}
.
By Lemma
C.15
,
\left\lVert P_{\mathbb{T}}E_{\omega}\right\rVert_{1}\leq C_{P}
and
\left\lVert\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}E_{\omega}\right\rVert_{1}\leq C_{P}
.
Therefore

\max_{\omega}\,\left\lVert(\hat{A}^{-1}\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}-A^{-1}P_{\mathbb{T}})E_{\omega}\right\rVert_{1}\leq 2C_{A}\,\sigma^{2}d^{\star}\cdot C_{P}.

(C.45)

Step 3: assembling.

Substituting (
C.45
) into (
C.44
):

\left\lVert\hat{H}-H^{\star}\right\rVert_{1}\leq 2C_{A}C_{P}\,\sigma^{2}d^{\star}\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1},

so

\frac{\left\lVert\hat{H}-H^{\star}\right\rVert_{1}}{d^{\star}}\leq 2C_{A}C_{P}\,\sigma^{2}\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}.

The second-order piece therefore satisfies

\boxed{\left|\tfrac{1}{2}\mathbb{P}^{\star}\big[\ddot{s}_{\eta}\left\langle\Delta,X\right\rangle^{2}\left\langle\hat{H}-H^{\star},X\right\rangle\big]\right|\leq C(\mu,r,m)\,C_{A}\,\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}.}

(C.46)

Note the key cancellations:

\sigma^{2}
from
\left\lVert A^{-1}\right\rVert_{1\to 1}\asymp\sigma^{2}d^{\star}
cancels with

1/\sigma^{2}
from
|\ddot{s}_{\eta}|\leq c_{2}/\sigma^{2}
,
and
d^{\star}
from
\left\lVert A^{-1}\right\rVert_{1\to 1}
cancels with

1/d^{\star}
from the
\ell_{1}
-average bound (Lemma
C.14
).

Remark C.17
.

The first-order piece (Proposition
C.16
) is bounded by

C(\mu,r,m)\,C_{A}\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\bigl[\left\lVert(I-\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}+\left\lVert(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}\bigr],

where the off-tangent components are controlled by the
\varepsilon^{2}
bound of Proposition
C.13
.
The second-order piece (
C.46
) is bounded by

C(\mu,r,m)\,C_{A}\,\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1},

which is also
O(\varepsilon^{2})
since
\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}=O(\varepsilon)
.
Both pieces retain explicit
C_{A}
dependence and are dimension-free up to
\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}
.

C.8
First-Order Cancellation

This is the heart of the one-step estimator: the plug-in bias
\langle P_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma,\Delta\rangle
cancels
exactly
with the leading term of the population correction
-\langle\Delta,G(H^{\star})\rangle
on the tangent space. The proof decomposes
\Delta=P_{\mathbb{T}}\Delta+(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta
: the tangent component cancels perfectly by the definition
H^{\star}=A^{-1}P_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma
, while the normal component produces a residual controlled by
\|(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta\|_{\infty}
, which is quadratic in subspace errors.

The combined first-order cancellation term from the bias decomposition is

\left\langle P_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma,\Delta\right\rangle+\mathbb{P}^{\star}\big[S_{\hat{T}}(H^{\star})-S_{T^{\star}}(H^{\star})\big].

(C.47)

C.8.1
Taylor expansion of the population score difference

The second part of (
C.47
) is

\mathbb{P}^{\star}\big[s_{\eta}(Y,\hat{\eta})\left\langle H^{\star},X\right\rangle\big]-\mathbb{P}^{\star}\big[s_{\eta}(Y,\eta^{\star})\left\langle H^{\star},X\right\rangle\big].

The second term vanishes (
\mathbb{E}[s_{\eta}|X]=0
). Expanding the first around
\eta^{\star}
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\star}\big[S_{\hat{T}}(H^{\star})-S_{T^{\star}}(H^{\star})\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}^{\star}\big[\dot{s}_{\eta}(Y,\eta^{\star})\left\langle\Delta,X\right\rangle\left\langle H^{\star},X\right\rangle\big]+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{P}^{\star}\big[\ddot{s}_{\eta}(Y,\bar{\eta})\left\langle\Delta,X\right\rangle^{2}\left\langle H^{\star},X\right\rangle\big]

\displaystyle=-\left\langle\Delta,G(H^{\star})\right\rangle+R_{2}(\Delta;H^{\star}),

(C.48)

where we used
\mathbb{E}[\dot{s}_{\eta}(Y,\eta^{\star})|X]=-I(\eta^{\star})
and defined

R_{2}(\Delta;H^{\star}):=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{P}^{\star}\big[\ddot{s}_{\eta}(Y,\bar{\eta})\left\langle\Delta,X\right\rangle^{2}\left\langle H^{\star},X\right\rangle\big].

C.8.2
The cancellation mechanism

Plugging (
C.48
) into (
C.47
):

\left\langle P_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma,\Delta\right\rangle-\left\langle\Delta,G(H^{\star})\right\rangle+R_{2}(\Delta;H^{\star}).

(C.49)

Now decompose
\Delta=P_{\mathbb{T}}\Delta+(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta
in the first-order part:

\displaystyle\left\langle P_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma,\Delta\right\rangle-\left\langle\Delta,G(H^{\star})\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\left\langle P_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma,P_{\mathbb{T}}\Delta\right\rangle-\left\langle P_{\mathbb{T}}\Delta,G(H^{\star})\right\rangle\Big)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\Big(\left\langle P_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma,(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta\right\rangle-\left\langle(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta,G(H^{\star})\right\rangle\Big).

The tangent-space part cancels exactly.

Since
H^{\star}=A^{-1}P_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma
with
A=P_{\mathbb{T}}GP_{\mathbb{T}}
:

\left\langle P_{\mathbb{T}}\Delta,G(H^{\star})\right\rangle=\left\langle P_{\mathbb{T}}\Delta,G(A^{-1}P_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma)\right\rangle=\left\langle P_{\mathbb{T}}\Delta,P_{\mathbb{T}}G(A^{-1}P_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma)\right\rangle+\left\langle P_{\mathbb{T}}\Delta,(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})G(H^{\star})\right\rangle.

The first term equals

\left\langle P_{\mathbb{T}}\Delta,A\cdot A^{-1}P_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma\right\rangle=\left\langle P_{\mathbb{T}}\Delta,P_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma\right\rangle,

which cancels with
\left\langle P_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma,P_{\mathbb{T}}\Delta\right\rangle
. The second term satisfies
\left\langle P_{\mathbb{T}}\Delta,(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})G(H^{\star})\right\rangle=0
by orthogonality.

The normal component survives but is small.

The surviving first-order term from (
C.8.2
) is

\left\langle P_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma,(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta\right\rangle-\left\langle(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta,G(H^{\star})\right\rangle.

(C.50)

C.8.3
Bounding the surviving first-order term

Proposition C.18
.

Under Assumption
C.5
,

\left|\left\langle P_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma,\Delta\right\rangle-\left\langle\Delta,G(H^{\star})\right\rangle\right|\leq C\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\,\left\lVert(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty},

(C.51)

where
C=C_{A}\cdot\mathrm{poly}(\mu,r,m)
(Remark
C.6
).

Proof of Proposition
C.18
.

For the first part of (
C.50
),

|\left\langle P_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma,(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta\right\rangle|=|\left\langle\Gamma,P_{\mathbb{T}}(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta\right\rangle|=0

since
P_{\mathbb{T}}(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})=0
.
For the second part, use symmetry of
G
and self-adjointness of
A^{-1}P_{\mathbb{T}}
to transfer the operator onto
\Gamma
:

\left\langle(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta,G(H^{\star})\right\rangle=\left\langle G(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta,A^{-1}P_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma\right\rangle=\left\langle A^{-1}P_{\mathbb{T}}G(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta,\Gamma\right\rangle.

By Hölder’s inequality,

\displaystyle\left|\left\langle A^{-1}P_{\mathbb{T}}G(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta,\Gamma\right\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\cdot\left\lVert A^{-1}P_{\mathbb{T}}G(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\cdot\left\lVert A^{-1}P_{\mathbb{T}}G\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\cdot\left\lVert(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}.

By Remark
C.6
,

\left\lVert A^{-1}P_{\mathbb{T}}G\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}=C_{A}\cdot\mathrm{poly}(\mu,r,m)
,
which gives (
C.51
).

\square

Remark C.19
.

Note
\left\langle P_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma,(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta\right\rangle=0
exactly, so the surviving term is
only

-\left\langle(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta,G(H^{\star})\right\rangle
, which is controlled by
\left\lVert(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}
. By Proposition
C.13
, this is quadratic in subspace errors (
O(\varepsilon^{2})
), which is why only the second-order remainder
R_{2}
survives as the dominant bias term.

C.9
Second-Order Score Remainder

Intuition.
After the first-order cancellation, the dominant bias term is the second-order Taylor remainder
R_{2}(\Delta;H^{\star})=O(\|\Delta\|_{\infty}^{2})
. The key mechanism is the
\ell_{1}
dimension cancellation:
\|A^{-1}P_{\mathbb{T}}E_{\omega}\|_{1}/d^{\star}\leq C_{A}\sigma^{2}\cdot\mathrm{poly}(\mu,r,m)
, where the
d^{\star}
in
\|A^{-1}\|_{1\to 1}\asymp\sigma^{2}d^{\star}
cancels with
1/d^{\star}
from the comparison averaging. This gives
|R_{2}|\leq C(\mu,r,m)C_{A}\|\Delta\|_{\infty}^{2}\|\Gamma\|_{1}
without any residual dimension factor.

The second-order score remainder from (
C.48
) is

R_{2}(\Delta;H^{\star})=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{P}^{\star}\big[\ddot{s}_{\eta}(Y,\bar{\eta})\left\langle\Delta,X\right\rangle^{2}\left\langle H^{\star},X\right\rangle\big].

Proposition C.20
(Second-order remainder bound).

Under Assumption
C.3
(with
|\ddot{s}_{\eta}|\leq c_{2}/\sigma^{2}
) and comparison design,

|R_{2}(\Delta;H^{\star})|\leq\frac{C}{\sigma^{2}}\,\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}\,\frac{\left\lVert H^{\star}\right\rVert_{1}}{d^{\star}}.

(C.52)

Expanding
H^{\star}=A^{-1}P_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma
and using
\left\lVert A^{-1}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\leq C_{A}\sigma^{2}d^{\star}
,
\left\lVert P_{\mathbb{T}}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}=\mathrm{poly}(\mu,r,m)
:

|R_{2}(\Delta;H^{\star})|\leq C(\mu,r,m)\,C_{A}\,\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}.

(C.53)

Proof of Proposition
C.20
.

Since
|\left\langle\Delta,X\right\rangle|\leq 2\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}
(comparison atom) and
|\ddot{s}_{\eta}|\leq c_{2}/\sigma^{2}
,

|R_{2}(\Delta;H^{\star})|\leq\frac{2c_{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\,\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}\,\mathbb{E}^{\star}\!\big[|\left\langle H^{\star},X\right\rangle|\big].

Under the comparison design,
\left\langle H^{\star},X\right\rangle=H^{\star}_{u,a}-H^{\star}_{u,b}
where
(u,\{a,b\})
is drawn uniformly, so by the triangle inequality and averaging,

\mathbb{E}^{\star}\!\big[|\left\langle H^{\star},X\right\rangle|\big]\leq\frac{2}{d^{\star}}\,\left\lVert H^{\star}\right\rVert_{1}
,
which gives (
C.52
) (absorbing the constant
4
into
C
).
Next, using
H^{\star}=A^{-1}P_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma=\sum_{\omega}\gamma_{\omega}A^{-1}P_{\mathbb{T}}E_{\omega}
and the triangle inequality,

\left\lVert H^{\star}\right\rVert_{1}\leq\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\cdot\max_{\omega}\left\lVert A^{-1}P_{\mathbb{T}}E_{\omega}\right\rVert_{1}.

For each
E_{\omega}
,

\left\lVert A^{-1}P_{\mathbb{T}}E_{\omega}\right\rVert_{1}\leq\left\lVert A^{-1}\right\rVert_{1\to 1}\,\left\lVert P_{\mathbb{T}}E_{\omega}\right\rVert_{1},

and by symmetry of
A^{-1}
,
\left\lVert A^{-1}\right\rVert_{1\to 1}=\left\lVert A^{-1}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}
.
By Assumption
C.5
,
\left\lVert A^{-1}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\leq C_{A}\,\sigma^{2}d^{\star}
, and since
P_{\mathbb{T}}
is symmetric,

\max_{\omega}\left\lVert P_{\mathbb{T}}E_{\omega}\right\rVert_{1}=\left\lVert P_{\mathbb{T}}\right\rVert_{1\to 1}=\left\lVert P_{\mathbb{T}}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}=\mathrm{poly}(\mu,r,m)

(Lemma
C.36
), which is dimension-free. Hence

\frac{1}{d^{\star}}\;\left\lVert A^{-1}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\cdot\max_{\omega}\left\lVert P_{\mathbb{T}}E_{\omega}\right\rVert_{1}\;=\;\frac{C_{A}\,\sigma^{2}d^{\star}}{d^{\star}}\cdot\mathrm{poly}(\mu,r,m)\;=\;C_{A}\,\sigma^{2}\cdot\mathrm{poly}(\mu,r,m).

The
\sigma^{2}
cancels the
1/\sigma^{2}
prefactor, yielding (
C.53
).

\square

Remark C.21
(
\ell_{1}
dimension cancellation).

The key mechanism is

\left\lVert A^{-1}P_{\mathbb{T}}E_{\omega}\right\rVert_{1}/d^{\star}\leq\left\lVert A^{-1}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\cdot\left\lVert P_{\mathbb{T}}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}/d^{\star}=C_{A}\,\sigma^{2}\cdot\mathrm{poly}(\mu,r,m).

The
d^{\star}
in
\left\lVert A^{-1}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\asymp\sigma^{2}d^{\star}
cancels exactly with the
1/d^{\star}
from the comparison averaging, and
\left\lVert P_{\mathbb{T}}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}=\mathrm{poly}(\mu,r,m)
is dimension-free.
This gives
|R_{2}|\leq C(\mu,r,m)\,C_{A}\,\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}
without any residual
\sqrt{\bar{d}}
factor.

C.10
Additional Error from Estimated Information
\hat{G}

As discussed in the proof strategy (Section
C.3
), the analysis in Sections
C.4
–
C.9
was carried out using the
true
information operator
G=\mathbb{E}^{\star}[I(\eta^{\star}(X))(X\otimes X)]
, i.e., the estimated direction was defined as
\hat{H}=(\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}G\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}})^{-1}\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma
.
In practice,
G
is replaced by the plug-in estimate
\hat{G}=\mathbb{E}^{\star}[I(\hat{\eta}(X))(X\otimes X)]
, yielding
\hat{A}^{\prime}:=\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}\hat{G}\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}
in place of
\hat{A}:=\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}G\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}
.
We now show that this substitution introduces only a negligible multiplicative correction.

Perturbation bound for
\hat{G}-G
.

The only difference between
\hat{G}
and
G
is the use of
I(\hat{\eta}(x))
in place of
I(\eta^{\star}(x))
.
Since the Fisher information
I(\cdot)
is Lipschitz on the bounded-signal domain (Assumption
C.3
), we have

|I(\hat{\eta}(x))-I(\eta^{\star}(x))|\;\leq\;L_{I}\,|\hat{\eta}(x)-\eta^{\star}(x)|\;=\;L_{I}\,|\left\langle\Delta,x\right\rangle|\;\leq\;2L_{I}\,\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}

for each comparison atom
x
, where
L_{I}
is the Lipschitz constant of
I
on
[-B-1,B+1]
.
Therefore

\|\hat{G}-G\|_{\infty\to\infty}\;\leq\;\frac{C\,\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}}{\sigma^{2}d^{\star}},

where we used
\|G\|_{\infty\to\infty}\asymp 1/(\sigma^{2}d^{\star})
.
Define the relative perturbation

\varepsilon_{G}\;:=\;\frac{\|\hat{G}-G\|_{\infty\to\infty}}{\|G\|_{\infty\to\infty}}\;\leq\;C\,\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}\;\leq\;C\,\sigma\sqrt{\frac{\bar{d}\log^{c}\bar{d}}{n}}\;\ll\;1.

(C.54)

Effect on the restricted inverse.

Write
\hat{A}^{\prime}=\hat{A}+\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}(\hat{G}-G)\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}
, so

\hat{A}^{\prime-1}\;=\;\hat{A}^{-1}\bigl(I+\hat{A}^{-1}\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}(\hat{G}-G)\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}\bigr)^{-1}.

The perturbation term satisfies

\|\hat{A}^{-1}\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}(\hat{G}-G)\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}\|_{\infty\to\infty}\leq\|\hat{A}^{-1}\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}G\|_{\infty\to\infty}\cdot\varepsilon_{G}\leq C(\mu,r,m)\cdot\varepsilon_{G}
,
where we used Remark
C.6
.
By the Neumann series, when
\varepsilon_{G}
is sufficiently small:

\hat{A}^{\prime-1}=\hat{A}^{-1}\bigl(I+O(\varepsilon_{G})\bigr)\quad\text{in }\|\cdot\|_{\infty\to\infty}.

Impact on each error term.

The replacement
\hat{A}\to\hat{A}^{\prime}
affects only the terms involving the estimated direction
\hat{H}
:

•

R_{\mathrm{emp}}^{H}
and the H-direction bias: the direction error
\hat{H}^{\prime}-H^{\star}
acquires a multiplicative
(1+O(\varepsilon_{G}))
correction. Since
\varepsilon_{G}=O(\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty})
is of the same order as the existing subspace estimation error, this is absorbed into the combined bound without changing the leading-order rate.

•

R_{\mathrm{emp}}^{\eta}
,
R_{\mathrm{proj}}
, the first-order cancellation, and the second-order remainder all involve only the
true
direction
H^{\star}
or the true operator
G
, so they receive
no additional error
from the
\hat{G}
substitution.

In summary, all bounds in this appendix remain valid with
\hat{G}
in place of
G
, up to a multiplicative
(1+O(\varepsilon_{G}))
factor that is negligible under the sample size condition.

C.11
Combined Bias Bound

We now collect all bias terms from Sections
C.6
–
C.9
and assemble the final bound.

C.11.1
Recap of individual bounds

The total bias decomposes as

\mathrm{Bias}=\underbrace{R_{\mathrm{proj}}}_{\text{Sec.~\ref{app:pd-Rproj}}}+\underbrace{\left\langle G\Delta,\hat{H}-H^{\star}\right\rangle}_{\text{H-bias 1st, Sec.~\ref{app:pd-H-bias}}}+\underbrace{\text{(H-bias 2nd)}}_{\text{Sec.~\ref{app:pd-H-bias}}}+\underbrace{\left\langle P_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma,\Delta\right\rangle-\left\langle\Delta,G(H^{\star})\right\rangle}_{\text{1st cancel, Sec.~\ref{app:pd-1st-cancel}}}+\underbrace{R_{2}(\Delta;H^{\star})}_{\text{2nd order, Sec.~\ref{app:pd-2nd-order}}}.

(C.55)

The individual bounds are:

(i)

Projection leakage
(Eq. (
C.32
)):

|R_{\mathrm{proj}}|\leq\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\cdot\left\lVert(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}
.

(ii)

H-direction bias, first order
(Prop.
C.16
):

|\left\langle G\Delta,\hat{H}-H^{\star}\right\rangle|\leq C(\mu,r,m)\,C_{A}\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\bigl[\left\lVert(I-\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}+\left\lVert(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}\bigr].

(iii)

First-order cancellation
(Prop.
C.18
):

|\left\langle P_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma,\Delta\right\rangle-\left\langle\Delta,G(H^{\star})\right\rangle|\leq C(\mu,r,m)\,C_{A}\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\,\left\lVert(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}.

(iv)

Second-order remainder
(Prop.
C.20
):

|R_{2}(\Delta;H^{\star})|\leq C(\mu,r,m)\,C_{A}\,\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}
.

(v)

H-direction bias, second order
(Eq. (
C.46
)):
controlled by
C\,C_{A}\,\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}
; same order as (iv).

C.11.2
Combined bound

Theorem C.22
(Combined bias bound).

Under Assumptions
C.3
and
C.5
, the total bias satisfies

|\mathrm{Bias}|\;\leq\;C(\mu,r,m)\,C_{A}\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\Big[\left\lVert(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}+\left\lVert(I-\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}+\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}\Big].

(C.56)

Proof of Theorem
C.22
.

Sum the bounds (i)–(iv): terms (i) and (iii) contribute
C\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\,\left\lVert(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}
;
term (ii) contributes
C\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}[\left\lVert(I-\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}+\left\lVert(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}]
;
term (iv) contributes
C\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\,\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}
.
Collecting with the maximum constant gives (
C.56
).

\square

C.11.3
Substituting the
\varepsilon^{2}
and entrywise rates

We now plug in the standard tensor estimation rates to obtain a fully explicit final bound.

Input rates.

Recall from Section
C.6.3
:

•

Normal-component error
(Proposition
C.13
):

\left\lVert(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty},\;\left\lVert(I-\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}\;\leq\;C(\mu,r,m)\,\rho^{2}
,
where
\rho:=\frac{\sigma}{\lambda_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{d^{\star}\,\bar{d}\log\bar{d}}{n}}
.

•

Entrywise error
:
\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq C(\mu,r,m)\cdot\sigma\sqrt{\bar{d}/n}
.

Substitution into each term.

(i)

First-order cancellation
(Prop.
C.18
):

C\,C_{A}\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\,\left\lVert(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}\;\leq\;C(\mu,r,m)\,C_{A}\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\,\rho^{2}\;=\;C(\mu,r,m)\,C_{A}\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\cdot\frac{\sigma^{2}\,d^{\star}\,\bar{d}\log\bar{d}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}\,n}.

(ii)

Second-order remainder
(Prop.
C.20
):

C\,C_{A}\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\,\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}\;\leq\;C(\mu,r,m)\,C_{A}\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\cdot\frac{\sigma^{2}\,\bar{d}}{n}.

(iii)

Projection leakage
(Eq. (
C.38
)):

|R_{\mathrm{proj}}|\;\leq\;C(\mu,r,m)\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\,\rho^{2}\;=\;C(\mu,r,m)\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\cdot\frac{\sigma^{2}\,d^{\star}\,\bar{d}\log\bar{d}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}\,n}.

(iv)

H-direction bias
(Prop.
C.16
):
controlled by

C\,C_{A}\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\bigl[\left\lVert(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}+\left\lVert(I-\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}+\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}\bigr]\leq C\,C_{A}\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\bigl[\rho^{2}+\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}\bigr].

Final explicit bound.

Under the SNR condition
\sigma/\lambda_{\min}\cdot\sqrt{d^{\star}}\asymp 1
, the
\rho^{2}
and
\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}
terms are the same order; both are retained.
Combining:

\boxed{|\mathrm{Bias}|\;\leq\;C(\mu,r,m)\,C_{A}\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\left[\frac{\sigma^{2}\,d^{\star}\,\bar{d}\log\bar{d}}{\lambda_{\min}^{2}\,n}+\frac{\sigma^{2}\,\bar{d}}{n}\right].}

(C.57)

Remark C.23
(Pairwise-comparison specialization).

In the pairwise-comparison setting,
\sigma=O(1)
and
\lambda_{\min}\asymp\sqrt{d^{\star}}
(Appendix
C.1
).
All bounds simplify because the two key estimation parameters collapse to the same scale:

\rho\;=\;\frac{\sigma}{\lambda_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{d^{\star}\bar{d}\log\bar{d}}{n}}\;\asymp\;\sqrt{\frac{\bar{d}\log^{c}\bar{d}}{n}},\qquad\delta_{\infty}\;:=\;\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}\;\lesssim\;\sqrt{\frac{\bar{d}\log^{c}\bar{d}}{n}}.

(C.58)

In particular, both
\rho
and
\delta_{\infty}
are of order
\sqrt{\bar{d}\log^{c}\bar{d}/n}
, and the products
\rho^{2}
,
\delta_{\infty}^{2}
,
\rho\cdot\delta_{\infty}
are all of order
\bar{d}\log^{c}\bar{d}/n
.

Substituting these rates into the combined bounds (
C.57
) and (
C.27
), with
\delta=\bar{d}^{-c}
(so
\log(2/\delta)\asymp c\log\bar{d}
), the total remainder satisfies

\boxed{|R_{n}|\;\leq\;C(\mu,r,m)\,C_{A}\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\,\frac{\bar{d}\log^{c}\bar{d}}{n}}

(C.59)

with probability
\geq 1-\bar{d}^{-c}
. This is the bound stated in Theorem
4.4
of the main text. Each of the six individual terms (
R_{\rm emp}^{H}
,
R_{\rm emp}^{\eta}
,
R_{\rm proj}
,
R_{\rm bias}^{H}
, first-order cancellation, second-order remainder) contributes at most this order, as summarized in Table
1
.

C.12
Range of
C_{A}
and dimension-free conditions

This subsection proves Proposition
4.6
: the coarse range
C(\mu,r,m)\leq C_{A}\leq C(\mu,r,m)\sqrt{\bar{d}}
, and the dimension-free regime under bounded signal. Throughout, we work under the bounded-signal assumption
\|T^{\star}\|_{\infty}\leq B
, so the Fisher information satisfies
c_{B}\leq I(\eta^{\star}(X))\leq C_{B}
for constants depending only on
B
.

Recall that
A^{-1}
acts only on the tangent space
\mathbb{T}
; accordingly, we define

\left\lVert A^{-1}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}:=\sup_{\|V\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\|A^{-1}P_{\mathbb{T}}V\|_{\infty}.

C.12.1
Spectral scale of the restricted inverse

Lemma C.24
(Spectral scale of
A
).

Under bounded signal,

\frac{c_{B}}{d^{\star}}\,P_{\mathbb{T}}\preceq A\preceq\frac{C_{B}}{d^{\star}}\,P_{\mathbb{T}}\qquad\text{on }\mathbb{T}.

Consequently,
\|A^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\asymp d^{\star}
, with constants depending only on
B
.

For any
H\in\mathbb{T}
,
\langle H,AH\rangle=\mathbb{E}_{\star}[I(\eta^{\star}(X))\langle H,X\rangle^{2}]
. The Fisher bounds give

c_{B}\,\mathbb{E}_{\star}\langle H,X\rangle^{2}\leq\langle H,AH\rangle\leq C_{B}\,\mathbb{E}_{\star}\langle H,X\rangle^{2}.

Under the uniform pairwise-comparison design, the Frobenius reduction (Lemma
C.30
) gives
\mathbb{E}_{\star}\langle H,X\rangle^{2}\asymp\|H\|_{F}^{2}/d^{\star}
, and the claim follows.
∎

C.12.2
Entrywise bound for tangent-space elements

The upper bound
C_{A}\lesssim\sqrt{\bar{d}}
is not a generic ambient-space norm inequality (which would only yield
\sqrt{d^{\star}}
). The sharper factor comes from the low-rank tangent-space geometry.

Lemma C.25
(Tangent-space entrywise bound).

Assume
\mu
-incoherence. For every
W\in\mathbb{T}
,

\|W\|_{\infty}\leq C(\mu,r,m)\sqrt{\frac{\bar{d}}{d^{\star}}}\,\|W\|_{F}.

Write the Tucker tangent-space decomposition
\mathbb{T}=\Pi_{\emptyset}(\mathcal{H})\oplus\bigoplus_{j=1}^{m}\Pi_{\{j\}}(\mathcal{H})
, where
\Pi_{\emptyset}
is the fully projected block and
\Pi_{\{j\}}
is the single-mode correction block. Any
W\in\mathbb{T}
decomposes orthogonally as
W=W_{\emptyset}+\sum_{j=1}^{m}W_{j}
.

For the main block
W_{\emptyset}
, every element lies in the span of tensors
a^{(1)}\otimes\cdots\otimes a^{(m)}
with
a^{(k)}\in\mathrm{col}(U_{k})
. By incoherence,
\|a^{(k)}\|_{\infty}\leq\sqrt{\mu r_{k}/d_{k}}\,\|a^{(k)}\|_{2}
, so for a rank-one tensor in this block,

\|a^{(1)}\otimes\cdots\otimes a^{(m)}\|_{\infty}\leq\prod_{k=1}^{m}\sqrt{\mu r_{k}/d_{k}}\;\prod_{k=1}^{m}\|a^{(k)}\|_{2}=C(\mu,r,m)\,(d^{\star})^{-1/2}\,\|a^{(1)}\otimes\cdots\otimes a^{(m)}\|_{F}.

By linearity,
\|W_{\emptyset}\|_{\infty}\leq C(\mu,r,m)\,(d^{\star})^{-1/2}\,\|W_{\emptyset}\|_{F}
.

For a single-mode block
W_{j}
, the range is generated by tensors with one free mode and all remaining modes projected. The free mode provides no incoherence gain (
\|b^{(j)}\|_{\infty}\leq\|b^{(j)}\|_{2}
), while each projected mode
k\neq j
still satisfies
\|a^{(k)}\|_{\infty}\leq\sqrt{\mu r_{k}/d_{k}}\,\|a^{(k)}\|_{2}
. Therefore

\|W_{j}\|_{\infty}\leq C(\mu,r,m)\Bigl(\prod_{k\neq j}d_{k}^{-1/2}\Bigr)\|W_{j}\|_{F}=C(\mu,r,m)\sqrt{\frac{d_{j}}{d^{\star}}}\,\|W_{j}\|_{F}\leq C(\mu,r,m)\sqrt{\frac{\bar{d}}{d^{\star}}}\,\|W_{j}\|_{F}.

Combining via the triangle inequality and the orthogonality of the
m+1
blocks (
m
is fixed) gives the claim.
∎

Remark C.26
.

This lemma is the precise source of the factor
\sqrt{\bar{d}}
: the free mode in each single-mode correction block introduces a
\sqrt{d_{j}}
factor that cannot be controlled by incoherence.

C.12.3
Coarse range of
C_{A}

Proposition C.27
(Coarse range of
C_{A}
).

Under bounded signal and
\mu
-incoherence,

c(\mu,r,m)\leq C_{A}\leq C(\mu,r,m)\sqrt{\bar{d}}.

Lower bound.
Since
\left\lVert M\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\geq\|M\|_{\mathrm{op}}
for any linear operator, Lemma
C.24
gives

\left\lVert A^{-1}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\geq\|A^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\geq c(B)\,d^{\star},

so
C_{A}=\left\lVert A^{-1}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}/d^{\star}\geq c(B)
.

Upper bound.
Let
V
satisfy
\|V\|_{\infty}\leq 1
and set
W:=A^{-1}P_{\mathbb{T}}V\in\mathbb{T}
. By Lemma
C.25
,

\|W\|_{\infty}\leq C(\mu,r,m)\sqrt{\frac{\bar{d}}{d^{\star}}}\,\|W\|_{F}.

Since
\|W\|_{F}\leq\|A^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|P_{\mathbb{T}}V\|_{F}\leq C(B)\,d^{\star}\cdot\sqrt{d^{\star}}
(using
\|V\|_{F}\leq\sqrt{d^{\star}}\,\|V\|_{\infty}
), we obtain

\left\lVert A^{-1}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\leq C(\mu,r,m)\sqrt{\frac{\bar{d}}{d^{\star}}}\cdot C(B)\,d^{\star}\sqrt{d^{\star}}=C(\mu,r,m,B)\,d^{\star}\sqrt{\bar{d}},

giving
C_{A}\leq C(\mu,r,m,B)\sqrt{\bar{d}}
.
∎

C.12.4
Dimension-free
C_{A}
under bounded signal

When the Fisher weights are nearly constant, a perturbative argument around the isotropic benchmark shows that
C_{A}
is dimension-free.

Proposition C.28
(Dimension-free
C_{A}
in the near-constant regime).

If
\|T^{\star}\|_{\infty}\leq B_{0}
where
B_{0}=B_{0}(\mu,m,r)
is sufficiently small depending only on the structural parameters, then

\left\lVert A^{-1}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\vee\left\lVert\widehat{A}^{-1}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\leq C(\mu,r,m,B_{0})\,d^{\star}.

In particular,
C_{A}\leq C(\mu,r,m,B_{0})
, a dimension-free constant.

Constant-weight benchmark.
Let
I_{0}:=I(0)=1/4
and define the benchmark operator

A_{0}:=P_{\mathbb{T}}G_{0}P_{\mathbb{T}},\qquad G_{0}(U):=\mathbb{E}_{\star}[I_{0}\langle U,X\rangle X].

Under constant Fisher weights, the information operator is isotropic on the identifiable comparison space:
A_{0}=(I_{0}/c_{\mathrm{pw}}d^{\star})\,P_{\mathbb{T}}
, where
c_{\mathrm{pw}}
is the pairwise-design normalization constant. Hence

A_{0}^{-1}=\frac{c_{\mathrm{pw}}d^{\star}}{I_{0}}\,P_{\mathbb{T}},\qquad\left\lVert A_{0}^{-1}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}=\frac{c_{\mathrm{pw}}d^{\star}}{I_{0}}\left\lVert P_{\mathbb{T}}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\leq C(\mu,r,m)\,d^{\star},

where the last step uses
\left\lVert P_{\mathbb{T}}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}=\mathrm{poly}(\mu,r,m)
(Lemma
C.36
).

Fisher-weight perturbation.
Since
I(\eta)
is smooth and even, for
|\eta|\leq 2B_{0}
we have
|I(\eta)-I_{0}|\leq L_{I}B_{0}
for an absolute constant
L_{I}
. Write

I(\eta^{\star}(X))=I_{0}+\delta(X),\qquad\|\delta\|_{\infty}\leq\varepsilon(B_{0})\to 0\text{ as }B_{0}\to 0.

Decompose
G=G_{0}+E
and
A=A_{0}+\Delta_{A}
with
\Delta_{A}:=P_{\mathbb{T}}EP_{\mathbb{T}}
. For the pairwise design, each design tensor
X
has two nonzero entries equal to
\pm 1
, so
|\langle U,X\rangle|\leq 2\|U\|_{\infty}
. Since the coordinate
\omega
is involved with probability
O(1/d^{\star})
,

\left\lVert E\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\leq C\,\varepsilon(B_{0})\,(d^{\star})^{-1},\qquad\left\lVert\Delta_{A}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\leq\left\lVert P_{\mathbb{T}}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}^{2}\left\lVert E\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\leq C(\mu,r,m)\,\varepsilon(B_{0})\,(d^{\star})^{-1}.

Neumann expansion.
The normalized perturbation satisfies

\rho:=\left\lVert A_{0}^{-1}\Delta_{A}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\leq\left\lVert A_{0}^{-1}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\left\lVert\Delta_{A}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\leq C(\mu,r,m)\,\varepsilon(B_{0}).

Choose
B_{0}
small enough that
\rho\leq 1/2
. Then
A=A_{0}(I+A_{0}^{-1}\Delta_{A})
with

\|(I+A_{0}^{-1}\Delta_{A})^{-1}\|_{\infty\to\infty}\leq\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\rho^{k}=\frac{1}{1-\rho}\leq 2,

so
\left\lVert A^{-1}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\leq 2\left\lVert A_{0}^{-1}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\leq C(\mu,r,m,B_{0})\,d^{\star}
.

Estimated operator.
The same argument applies to
\widehat{A}=\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}\widehat{G}\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}
on the high-probability event that

\left\lVert\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\leq C(\mu,r,m),\qquad\left\lVert\widehat{G}-G\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}=o((d^{\star})^{-1}).

∎

C.13
Relative error of plug-in variance estimators

This subsection proves the variance estimation bounds (
4.14
) and (
5.9
).

C.13.1
Algebraic reduction to relative direction error

The following lemma applies to both the efficient and whitened settings.

Lemma C.29
(Variance difference reduces to direction error).

Let
\hat{\phi}(X,Y)=s_{\star}(Y,X)\langle\widehat{H},X\rangle
and
\phi^{\star}(X,Y)=s_{\star}(Y,X)\langle H^{\star},X\rangle
, where
s_{\star}
denotes either the usual score
s_{\eta}(Y,\eta^{\star})
or the whitened score
\tilde{s}(Y,\eta^{\star})
. Assume the score-squared factor satisfies
\mathbb{E}^{\star}[s_{\star}^{2}f(X)]\asymp\mathbb{E}^{\star}[f(X)]
for nonnegative
f
. If the relative direction error

\delta_{H}:=\frac{\|\widehat{H}-H^{\star}\|_{F}}{\|H^{\star}\|_{F}}

is sufficiently small, then the direction-induced relative variance error satisfies

\frac{\bigl|\mathbb{P}^{\star}\bigl[s_{\star}^{2}(\langle\widehat{H},X\rangle^{2}-\langle H^{\star},X\rangle^{2})\bigr]\bigr|}{\mathbb{P}^{\star}[s_{\star}^{2}\langle H^{\star},X\rangle^{2}]}\;\lesssim\;\delta_{H}.

Write
a:=\langle\widehat{H},X\rangle
and
b:=\langle H^{\star},X\rangle
. By score comparability,
|\mathbb{P}^{\star}[s_{\star}^{2}(a^{2}-b^{2})]|\lesssim\mathbb{P}^{\star}|a^{2}-b^{2}|
. The factorization
a^{2}-b^{2}=(a-b)(a+b)
and Cauchy–Schwarz give

\mathbb{P}^{\star}|a^{2}-b^{2}|\leq\bigl(\mathbb{P}^{\star}(a-b)^{2}\bigr)^{1/2}\bigl(\mathbb{P}^{\star}(a+b)^{2}\bigr)^{1/2}.

By the pairwise Frobenius reduction (Lemma
C.30
),

\mathbb{P}^{\star}(a-b)^{2}\asymp\|\widehat{H}-H^{\star}\|_{F}^{2}/d^{\star},\qquad\mathbb{P}^{\star}b^{2}\asymp\|H^{\star}\|_{F}^{2}/d^{\star}.

When
\delta_{H}\leq c_{0}
,
(a+b)^{2}\leq 2(a-b)^{2}+8b^{2}
gives
\mathbb{P}^{\star}(a+b)^{2}\leq 10\,\mathbb{P}^{\star}b^{2}
. Substituting and dividing by
\mathbb{P}^{\star}[s_{\star}^{2}b^{2}]\asymp\mathbb{P}^{\star}b^{2}
yields the claim.
∎

C.13.2
Efficient estimator: proof of (
4.14
)

The total relative variance error decomposes into three parts: empirical fluctuation, score plug-in, and direction-induced.

C.13.2.1
Direction-induced part.

The efficient directions satisfy
H^{\star}=A^{-1}P_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma
and
\widehat{H}=\widehat{A}^{-1}\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma
. Add and subtract
A^{-1}\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma
and use the resolvent identity
\widehat{A}^{-1}-A^{-1}=A^{-1}(A-\widehat{A})\widehat{A}^{-1}
:

\widehat{H}-H^{\star}=\underbrace{A^{-1}(\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Gamma}_{T_{1}}+\underbrace{A^{-1}(A-\widehat{A})\widehat{A}^{-1}\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma}_{T_{2}}.

Since
A
has spectral scale
(d^{\star})^{-1}
on the tangent space,
A^{-1}
multiplies Frobenius norms by
\asymp d^{\star}
. This common factor cancels in the relative ratio
\|T_{1}\|_{F}/\|H^{\star}\|_{F}
, yielding

\frac{\|T_{1}\|_{F}}{\|H^{\star}\|_{F}}\lesssim\frac{\|(\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Gamma\|_{F}}{\|P_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma\|_{F}}.

Under the sparse-target projector perturbation bound
\|(\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Gamma\|_{F}\lesssim\rho\sqrt{\bar{d}/d^{\star}}\,\|\Gamma\|_{F}
and the alignment condition
\|P_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma\|_{F}\geq\alpha_{\Gamma}\sqrt{\bar{d}/d^{\star}}\,\|\Gamma\|_{F}
, this is
O(\rho/\alpha_{\Gamma})
. For
T_{2}
, the operator perturbation
\|A-\widehat{A}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\lesssim\rho/(d^{\star})
together with
\|A^{-1}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\asymp d^{\star}
gives
\|A^{-1}(A-\widehat{A})\|_{\mathrm{op}}\lesssim\rho
, so
\|T_{2}\|_{F}\lesssim\rho\|\widehat{H}\|_{F}\asymp\rho\|H^{\star}\|_{F}
. Combining,

\delta_{H}:=\frac{\|\widehat{H}-H^{\star}\|_{F}}{\|H^{\star}\|_{F}}\lesssim\frac{\rho}{\alpha_{\Gamma}}.

Lemma
C.29
then gives a direction-induced relative error of
O(\rho/\alpha_{\Gamma})
.

C.13.2.2
Score plug-in part.

By the derivative bound on the score,
|s_{\eta}(Y,\hat{\eta})^{2}-s_{\eta}(Y,\eta^{\star})^{2}|\lesssim\|\widehat{T}-T^{\star}\|_{\infty}
, so the relative contribution is
O(\|\widehat{T}-T^{\star}\|_{\infty})
.

C.13.2.3
Empirical fluctuation.

Conditional on the first-stage sample,
\widehat{V}_{\rm eff}-\mathbb{P}^{\star}[\hat{\phi}^{2}]=(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mathbb{P}^{\star})(\hat{\phi}^{2})
is a mean-zero average. A Bernstein bound gives relative error
O(\sqrt{\bar{d}\log\bar{d}/n})
.

Combining the three parts yields (
4.14
).

C.13.3
Score-whitened estimator: proof of (
5.9
)

The argument simplifies because the whitened directions
H_{\rm ws}^{\star}=d^{\star}P_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma
and
\widehat{H}_{\rm ws}=d^{\star}\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma
involve no operator inversion. The direction difference is

\widehat{H}_{\rm ws}-H_{\rm ws}^{\star}=d^{\star}(\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Gamma,

so the
d^{\star}
factor cancels in the relative ratio:

\frac{\|\widehat{H}_{\rm ws}-H_{\rm ws}^{\star}\|_{F}}{\|H_{\rm ws}^{\star}\|_{F}}=\frac{\|(\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Gamma\|_{F}}{\|P_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma\|_{F}}\lesssim\frac{\rho}{\alpha_{\Gamma}}.

Lemma
C.29
(applied with the whitened score
\tilde{s}
) gives the direction-induced relative error. The score plug-in and empirical fluctuation parts are identical to the efficient case, completing the proof of (
5.9
).

C.14
Useful Technical Tools

C.14.1
Frobenius reduction for comparison sampling

Lemma C.30
(Pairwise difference identity).

Fix any vector
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}
with
\sum_{a=1}^{d_{1}}z_{a}=0
. If
\{a,b\}
is uniformly distributed over unordered pairs, then

\mathbb{E}_{\{a,b\}}\big[(z_{a}-z_{b})^{2}\big]=\frac{2}{d_{1}-1}\,\left\lVert z\right\rVert_{2}^{2}.

(C.60)

Proof of Lemma
C.30
.

We have
\sum_{a<b}(z_{a}-z_{b})^{2}=d_{1}\sum_{a}z_{a}^{2}-(\sum_{a}z_{a})^{2}=d_{1}\left\lVert z\right\rVert_{2}^{2}
.
Dividing by
\binom{d_{1}}{2}
yields (
C.60
).

\square

Lemma C.31
(Frobenius reduction for comparison sampling).

Let
H
satisfy the column-sum-zero condition (
C.5
). Under the comparison sampling model,

\mathbb{E}^{\star}\big[\left\langle H,X\right\rangle^{2}\big]=\frac{2}{D(d_{1}-1)}\,\left\lVert H\right\rVert_{F}^{2}\asymp\frac{\left\lVert H\right\rVert_{F}^{2}}{d^{\star}}.

(C.61)

Proof of Lemma
C.31
.

Write
h_{u}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}}
for the
u
-th context slice.
Conditioning on
u
and applying Lemma
C.30
:

\mathbb{E}^{\star}[\left\langle H,X\right\rangle^{2}]=\frac{1}{D}\sum_{u}\frac{2}{d_{1}-1}\left\lVert h_{u}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}=\frac{2}{D(d_{1}-1)}\left\lVert H\right\rVert_{F}^{2}
.

\square

Corollary C.32
(Weighted second moment).

Under Assumption
C.3
and Lemma
C.31
, for any column-sum-zero
H
,

\mathbb{E}^{\star}\!\big[I(\eta^{\star}(X))\,\left\langle H,X\right\rangle^{2}\big]\leq\frac{c_{+}}{\sigma^{2}}\cdot\frac{\left\lVert H\right\rVert_{F}^{2}}{d^{\star}}.

(C.62)

C.14.2
\ell_{1}
extraction

Lemma C.33
(
\ell_{1}
extraction).

For any linear map
\mathcal{M}
and any
\Gamma=\sum_{\omega}\gamma_{\omega}e_{\omega}
,

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{M}\,\Gamma\right\rVert_{F}\leq\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\ \max_{\omega}\ \left\lVert\mathcal{M}e_{\omega}\right\rVert_{F},

(C.63)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{M}\,\Gamma\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\ \max_{\omega}\ \left\lVert\mathcal{M}e_{\omega}\right\rVert_{\infty},

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{M}\,\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\leq\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\ \max_{\omega}\ \left\lVert\mathcal{M}e_{\omega}\right\rVert_{1}.

Proof of Lemma
C.33
.

By linearity and the triangle inequality,

\left\lVert\mathcal{M}\Gamma\right\rVert_{F}\leq\sum_{\omega}|\gamma_{\omega}|\,\left\lVert\mathcal{M}e_{\omega}\right\rVert_{F}\leq\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\,\max_{\omega}\left\lVert\mathcal{M}e_{\omega}\right\rVert_{F}
.
The
\ell_{\infty}
and
\ell_{1}
versions are identical.

\square

C.14.3
Block projections and tangent space for Tucker structure

Let
\mathcal{H}:=\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times\cdots\times d_{m}}
with Frobenius inner product.
Fix orthonormal matrices
U_{j}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{j}\times r_{j}}
and define
P_{j}:=U_{j}U_{j}^{\top}
,
P_{j}^{\perp}:=I_{d_{j}}-P_{j}
.
For any subset
S\subseteq[m]
, define the
block projector

\Pi_{S}(X):=X\times_{j\in S}P_{j}^{\perp}\times_{k\notin S}P_{k}.

(C.64)

Lemma C.34
(Orthogonal block decomposition).

The family
\{\Pi_{S}\}_{S\subseteq[m]}
consists of orthogonal projections with pairwise orthogonal ranges and
\sum_{S\subseteq[m]}\Pi_{S}=I
.

Proof of Lemma
C.34
.

Each
P_{j},P_{j}^{\perp}
is symmetric idempotent, and products in distinct modes commute. If
S\neq S^{\prime}
, pick
j\in S\setminus S^{\prime}
; then
\Pi_{S}
multiplies by
P_{j}^{\perp}
and
\Pi_{S^{\prime}}
by
P_{j}
, giving
\Pi_{S}\Pi_{S^{\prime}}=0
.
For the resolution,
\prod_{j}(P_{j}+P_{j}^{\perp})=I
, and expanding yields
\sum_{S}\Pi_{S}
.

\square

Lemma C.35
(Tangent space is “
|S|\leq 1
” blocks).

Under full multilinear rank,

\mathcal{T}=\Pi_{\emptyset}(\mathcal{H})\;\oplus\;\bigoplus_{j=1}^{m}\Pi_{\{j\}}(\mathcal{H}),\qquad\mathcal{T}^{\perp}=\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}S\subseteq[m]\\
|S|\geq 2\end{subarray}}\Pi_{S}(\mathcal{H}).

(C.65)

In particular,
(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta=\sum_{|S|\geq 2}\Pi_{S}(\Delta)
.

Proof of Lemma
C.35
.

For the inclusion
\subseteq
: each term in the tangent space representation
\delta C\times_{1}U_{1}\cdots\times_{m}U_{m}
lies in
\Pi_{\emptyset}(\mathcal{H})
, and each
C^{\star}\times_{j}W_{j}\times_{k\neq j}U_{k}
(with
U_{j}^{\top}W_{j}=0
) lies in
\Pi_{\{j\}}(\mathcal{H})
.
For the inclusion
\supseteq
: any
Y\in\Pi_{\emptyset}(\mathcal{H})
can be written as
\tilde{C}\times_{1}U_{1}\cdots\times_{m}U_{m}\in\mathcal{T}
, and any
Y\in\Pi_{\{j\}}(\mathcal{H})
has mode-
j
fibers in
\mathrm{Range}(P_{j}^{\perp})
; by full rank of
C^{\star}_{(j)}
, one can find
W_{j}
with
U_{j}^{\top}W_{j}=0
such that
Y=C^{\star}\times_{j}W_{j}\times_{k\neq j}U_{k}\in\mathcal{T}
.
The orthogonal complement formula follows from Lemma
C.34
.

\square

C.14.4
Dimension-free
\infty\!\to\!\infty
bounds for projections

Lemma C.36
(Dimension-free bounds).

Let
P=UU^{\top}
with
\max_{i}\|e_{i}^{\top}U\|_{2}^{2}\leq\mu r/d
. Then

\left\lVert P\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\leq\sqrt{\mu}\,r,\qquad\left\lVert P^{\perp}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\leq 1+\sqrt{\mu}\,r.

(C.66)

For the centering
Q=I-\frac{1}{d}\mathbf{1}\mathbf{1}^{\top}
:
\left\lVert Q\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}<2
and
\left\lVert Q-P\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\leq 2+\sqrt{\mu}\,r
.

For Kronecker products:
\left\lVert\bigotimes_{j}A_{j}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}=\prod_{j}\left\lVert A_{j}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}
.
For the tangent projector:
\left\lVert P_{\mathbb{T}}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\leq\sum_{|S|\leq 1}\left\lVert\Pi_{S}\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}=\mathrm{poly}(\mu,r,m)
.

Proof of Lemma
C.36
.

Row
i
of
P
has entries
P_{ij}=\left\langle u_{i},u_{j}\right\rangle
, so
\sum_{j}|P_{ij}|\leq\|u_{i}\|_{2}\sqrt{d}\sqrt{r}=\sqrt{\mu r/d}\cdot\sqrt{dr}=\sqrt{\mu}\,r
.
For
P^{\perp}
,
\left\lVert I-P\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}\leq 1+\left\lVert P\right\rVert_{\infty\to\infty}
.
For
Q
, each row has sum
|1-1/d|+(d-1)/d=2-2/d<2
.
For Kronecker products, absolute row sums multiply.

C.14.5
\|P_{\mathbb{T}}E_{\omega}\|_{\infty}
bound

Lemma C.37
(
\|P_{\mathbb{T}}E_{\omega}\|_{\infty}
is
d/d^{\star}
up to incoherence).

Under
\mu
-incoherence, for any canonical basis tensor
E_{\omega}=e_{i_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes e_{i_{m}}
,

\left\lVert P_{\mathbb{T}}E_{\omega}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq C_{\mu}\,\frac{d}{d^{\star}},\qquad C_{\mu}:=(m+1)\mu^{m},

(C.67)

where
d:=\prod_{j}r_{j}(1+\sum_{k}d_{k}/r_{k})
is the effective tangent dimension proxy.

Proof of Lemma
C.37
.

Using
P_{\mathbb{T}}=\Pi_{\emptyset}+\sum_{k}\Pi_{\{k\}}
, we have

\left\lVert\Pi_{\emptyset}(E_{\omega})\right\rVert_{\infty}=\prod_{j}\left\lVert P_{j}e_{i_{j}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\mu^{m}\prod_{j}r_{j}/d_{j}=\mu^{m}r^{\star}/d^{\star},

and

\left\lVert\Pi_{\{k\}}(E_{\omega})\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\left\lVert P_{k}^{\perp}e_{i_{k}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\prod_{j\neq k}\left\lVert P_{j}e_{i_{j}}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\mu^{m-1}(r^{\star}/d^{\star})(d_{k}/r_{k}).

Summing gives

\left\lVert P_{\mathbb{T}}E_{\omega}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\mu^{m}r^{\star}/d^{\star}+\sum_{k}\mu^{m-1}r^{\star}d_{k}/(r_{k}d^{\star})\leq(m+1)\mu^{m}\cdot d/d^{\star}.

\square

Sharp
\|P_{\mathbb{T}}\omega\|_{\infty}
for comparison design.

For the comparison design,
\omega=e_{u,a}-e_{u,b}
and the Ma-type tangent projector decomposition
P_{\mathbb{T}}=\mathcal{P}_{\times}+\sum_{j}\mathcal{R}_{j}
gives, via the rank-one unfolding argument:

\|P_{\mathbb{T}}\omega\|_{\infty}\leq\mathrm{poly}(\mu,r,m)\cdot\frac{d_{\max}}{d^{\star}}.

(C.68)

The main term
\mathcal{P}_{\times}(\omega)
factorizes as
\bigotimes_{k}P_{U_{k}}e_{i_{k}}
, giving
\mu^{m}r^{\star}/d^{\star}
.
Each correction
\mathcal{R}_{j}(\omega)
is rank-one in mode-
j
matricization with

\|\mathcal{R}_{j}(\omega)\|_{\infty}\leq(1+\mu r_{j})\mu^{m-1}r^{\star}d_{j}/(r_{j}d^{\star})
.
The additional row-sum projection
\Pi_{k}
satisfies
\|\Pi_{k}(Z)\|_{\infty}\leq 2\|Z\|_{\infty}
and does not affect the
d^{\star}
cancellation.

C.14.6
Symmetry and norm equivalences

Lemma C.38
(Symmetry:
\|M\|_{1\to 1}=\|M\|_{\infty\to\infty}
).

For any
M\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times p}
,
\|M\|_{1\to 1}=\|M^{\top}\|_{\infty\to\infty}
.
If
M=M^{\top}
, then
\|M\|_{1\to 1}=\|M\|_{\infty\to\infty}
.

C.14.7
Bernstein template

Lemma C.39
(Bernstein for bounded variables).

Let
W_{1},\dots,W_{n}
be i.i.d. mean-zero with
|W_{i}|\leq M
a.s. and
\mathrm{Var}(W_{i})\leq v
.
Then for any
\delta\in(0,1)
, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}W_{i}\right|\leq C\left(\sqrt{\frac{v\log(2/\delta)}{n}}+\frac{M\log(2/\delta)}{n}\right).

C.14.8
Entrywise contraction

Lemma C.40
(Entrywise contraction via
\|\cdot\|_{2,\infty}
).

Let
G\in\mathbb{R}^{r_{1}\times\cdots\times r_{m}}
and
A_{j}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{j}\times r_{j}}
.
Then
\left\lVert G\times_{1}A_{1}\cdots\times_{m}A_{m}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\left\lVert G\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\prod_{j}\left\lVert A_{j}\right\rVert_{2,\infty}
.

Proof of Lemma
C.40
.

For each entry
(i_{1},\dots,i_{m})
, the value is
\left\langle G,a_{1}(i_{1})\otimes\cdots\otimes a_{m}(i_{m})\right\rangle
where
a_{j}(i_{j})^{\top}=e_{i_{j}}^{\top}A_{j}
.
By definition of
\left\lVert G\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}
,

|Y_{i_{1},\dots,i_{m}}|\leq\left\lVert G\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\prod_{j}\|a_{j}(i_{j})\|_{2}\leq\left\lVert G\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\prod_{j}\left\lVert A_{j}\right\rVert_{2,\infty}.

\square

D
Proofs for Section
5

This appendix proves Theorems
5.1
and
5.2
.
The proof follows the same five-term decomposition as Appendix
C
, but each term simplifies dramatically because score whitening turns the information operator into
(1/d^{\star})I
, eliminating the operator inverse and
C_{A}
entirely.

Proof roadmap and comparison with the general case.

For ease of reference, we list the five remainder terms side-by-side:

Term

General (Sec.
C
)

Whitened (this section)

Direction error
R_{\rm emp}^{H}

C_{A}\sigma\sqrt{\bar{d}}\,\rho\sqrt{\log/n}

\sqrt{\bar{d}}\,\rho\sqrt{\log/n}

Score perturbation
R_{\rm emp}^{\eta}

C_{A}\,\delta_{\infty}\sqrt{\bar{d}/n}

\delta_{\infty}\sqrt{\bar{d}/n}

Projection leakage
R_{\rm proj}

\rho^{2}

\rho^{2}
(unchanged)

H-bias (1st order)

C_{A}\,\rho^{2}

\rho^{2}

1st-order cancel + 2nd remainder

C_{A}\,\rho^{2}+C_{A}\,\delta_{\infty}^{2}

0+\delta_{\infty}^{2}

The key simplifications are: (i)
C_{A}
disappears from all terms because
A_{0}^{-1}=d^{\star}P_{\mathbb{T}}
is explicit; (ii) the
\sigma
factors from
\|A^{-1}\|
and
1/\sigma
from the score cancel automatically since the whitened score has
O(1)
bounds; (iii) the first-order cancellation becomes
complete
(zero, not just
O(\rho^{2})
), because
G_{0}(H^{\star}_{\rm ws})=P_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma\in\mathcal{T}
has no off-tangent component.

D.1
Setup and notation

We adopt the comparison sampling model and column-sum-zero restriction of Section
C.2
.
Throughout,
\tilde{s}(y,\eta):=s(y,\eta)/I(\eta)
denotes the whitened score (Eq. (
5.1
)).
Its key properties, inherited from the exponential family structure, are:

(i)

Centering:

\mathbb{E}^{\star}[\tilde{s}(Y,\eta^{\star})\mid X]=0
.

(ii)

Constant conditional derivative:

\mathbb{E}^{\star}[\partial_{\eta}\tilde{s}(Y,\eta^{\star})\mid X]=-1
, regardless of
X
.

Whitened score regularity.

From Assumption
C.3
, the original score satisfies
|s(y,\eta)|\leq C_{\psi_{1}}\asymp 1/\sigma
and
I(\eta)\geq c_{-}/\sigma^{2}
.
Therefore the whitened score satisfies

|\tilde{s}(y,\eta)|=\frac{|s(y,\eta)|}{I(\eta)}\leq\frac{C_{\psi_{1}}}{c_{-}/\sigma^{2}}=\frac{C_{\psi_{1}}\sigma^{2}}{c_{-}}\leq C_{\tilde{s}},

(D.1)

where
C_{\tilde{s}}:=C_{\psi_{1}}\sigma^{2}/c_{-}
is a bounded constant (since
\sigma
is a constant in the BTL model).
Similarly, by Assumption
C.3
(i),

|\partial_{\eta}\tilde{s}(y,\eta)|\leq\frac{|\dot{s}(y,\eta)|}{I(\eta)}+\frac{|s(y,\eta)|\cdot|I^{\prime}(\eta)|}{I(\eta)^{2}}\leq C_{\dot{\tilde{s}}},

(D.2)

and the second derivative
|\partial^{2}_{\eta}\tilde{s}(y,\eta)|\leq C_{\ddot{\tilde{s}}}
, where
C_{\dot{\tilde{s}}},C_{\ddot{\tilde{s}}}
are dimension-free constants depending only on
(c_{-},c_{+},c_{1},c_{2},C_{\psi_{1}},\sigma)
.
In particular,
\|\tilde{s}(Y,\hat{\eta})\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq C_{\tilde{s}}
(the whitened score is bounded, hence sub-exponential with a
constant
parameter, unlike the original score which has
\psi_{1}
-norm
\asymp 1/\sigma
).

Isotropic Gram operator.

Under the whitened score and uniform comparison design, by property (ii) the effective Gram operator is

G_{0}(H)=\mathbb{E}^{\star}\!\big[\left\langle H,X\right\rangle\,X\big]=\frac{1}{d^{\star}}\,H

(D.3)

on the column-sum-zero subspace (Lemma
C.31
).
The restricted operators are therefore

A_{0}=P_{\mathbb{T}}G_{0}P_{\mathbb{T}}=\frac{1}{d^{\star}}\,P_{\mathbb{T}},\qquad A_{0}^{-1}=d^{\star}\,P_{\mathbb{T}}.

Simplified directions.

The oracle and estimated EIF directions are

H^{\star}_{\rm ws}=d^{\star}\,P_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma,\qquad\hat{H}_{\rm ws}=d^{\star}\,\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma,

(D.4)

and the direction error is

\hat{H}_{\rm ws}-H^{\star}_{\rm ws}=d^{\star}\,(\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Gamma.

(D.5)

No operator inversion, Neumann series expansion, or Gram mismatch analysis is needed.

D.2
Bounding
R_{\mathrm{emp}}^{\tilde{H}}
(direction-error empirical process)

Define

R_{\mathrm{emp}}^{\tilde{H}}:=(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mathbb{P}^{\star})\big[\tilde{s}(Y,\hat{\eta})\,\left\langle\hat{H}_{\rm ws}-H^{\star}_{\rm ws},X\right\rangle\big].

Let
Z_{i}:=\tilde{s}(Y_{i},\hat{\eta}_{i})\,\left\langle\hat{H}_{\rm ws}-H^{\star}_{\rm ws},X_{i}\right\rangle
.
We bound its variance and sub-exponential norm following the five-step template of Section
C.4
.

D.2.1
Step 1: Variance bound via Frobenius reduction

Since
|\tilde{s}(Y,\hat{\eta})|\leq C_{\tilde{s}}
(Eq. (
D.1
)),

\mathbb{E}^{\star}[Z_{i}^{2}]\leq C_{\tilde{s}}^{2}\,\mathbb{E}^{\star}\!\big[\left\langle\hat{H}_{\rm ws}-H^{\star}_{\rm ws},X\right\rangle^{2}\big].

Applying the Frobenius reduction (Lemma
C.31
):

\mathrm{Var}(Z_{i})\leq\mathbb{E}^{\star}[Z_{i}^{2}]\leq C_{\tilde{s}}^{2}\cdot\frac{\left\lVert\hat{H}_{\rm ws}-H^{\star}_{\rm ws}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}}{d^{\star}}.

(D.6)

Note that the Fisher weight
c_{+}/\sigma^{2}
from the general case is now replaced by the constant
C_{\tilde{s}}^{2}
.

D.2.2
Step 2: Extracting
\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}
from the Frobenius norm

Since
\hat{H}_{\rm ws}-H^{\star}_{\rm ws}=d^{\star}\,(\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Gamma
, by Lemma
C.33
:

\left\lVert\hat{H}_{\rm ws}-H^{\star}_{\rm ws}\right\rVert_{F}=d^{\star}\,\left\lVert(\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Gamma\right\rVert_{F}\leq d^{\star}\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\cdot\max_{\omega}\left\lVert(\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}-P_{\mathbb{T}})E_{\omega}\right\rVert_{F}.

(D.7)

D.2.3
Step 3: Bounding
\max_{\omega}\left\lVert(\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}-P_{\mathbb{T}})E_{\omega}\right\rVert_{F}

By the subspace estimation guarantee (Eq. (
C.18
)):

\max_{\omega}\left\lVert(\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}-P_{\mathbb{T}})E_{\omega}\right\rVert_{F}\leq C(\mu,r,m)\cdot\frac{\sqrt{\bar{d}}\,\rho}{\sqrt{d^{\star}}},

(D.8)

where
\rho=\lambda_{\min}^{-1}\sqrt{d^{\star}\bar{d}\log\bar{d}/n}
as in (
C.35
).
This is the
same bound
as in the general case (Eq. (
C.18
)), with the
\sqrt{\bar{d}}
arising from the
(I-P_{U})
mode in the tangent space projector.
The key simplification is that
no resolvent decomposition
(Eq. (
C.16
)) is needed—the direction error is a single term
d^{\star}(\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Gamma
rather than the two-term expansion
A^{-1}(\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}-P_{\mathbb{T}})+A^{-1}(A-\hat{A})\hat{A}^{-1}\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}
.

Substituting (
D.8
) into (
D.7
):

\left\lVert\hat{H}_{\rm ws}-H^{\star}_{\rm ws}\right\rVert_{F}\leq d^{\star}\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\cdot C(\mu,r,m)\cdot\frac{\sqrt{\bar{d}}\,\rho}{\sqrt{d^{\star}}}=C(\mu,r,m)\,\sqrt{d^{\star}\bar{d}}\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\,\rho.

Plugging into (
D.6
):

\mathrm{Var}(Z_{i})\leq C(\mu,r,m)\,\bar{d}\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}^{2}\,\rho^{2}.

(D.9)

The
C_{A}^{2}\sigma^{2}
prefactor from the general case has been absorbed into
C(\mu,r,m)
; the
\bar{d}
factor persists from the uncompressed mode in the tangent projector.

D.2.4
Step 4: Sub-exponential (
\psi_{1}
) bound via
\ell_{\infty}
norm

Since
|\tilde{s}|\leq C_{\tilde{s}}
(bounded, hence sub-exponential with
\|\tilde{s}\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq C_{\tilde{s}}
) and
|\left\langle\hat{H}_{\rm ws}-H^{\star}_{\rm ws},X\right\rangle|\leq 2\left\lVert\hat{H}_{\rm ws}-H^{\star}_{\rm ws}\right\rVert_{\infty}
:

\|Z_{i}\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq 2C_{\tilde{s}}\,\left\lVert\hat{H}_{\rm ws}-H^{\star}_{\rm ws}\right\rVert_{\infty}.

(D.10)

By Lemma
C.33
(entrywise version) and the
P_{\mathbb{T}}E_{\omega}
bound (Lemma
C.37
):

\left\lVert\hat{H}_{\rm ws}-H^{\star}_{\rm ws}\right\rVert_{\infty}=d^{\star}\,\left\lVert(\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Gamma\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq d^{\star}\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\cdot\max_{\omega}\left\lVert(\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}-P_{\mathbb{T}})E_{\omega}\right\rVert_{\infty}.

Since

\left\lVert(\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}-P_{\mathbb{T}})E_{\omega}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\left\lVert\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}E_{\omega}\right\rVert_{\infty}+\left\lVert P_{\mathbb{T}}E_{\omega}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq 2C(\mu,r,m)\,\bar{d}/d^{\star}

(Lemma
C.37
):

\left\lVert\hat{H}_{\rm ws}-H^{\star}_{\rm ws}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq d^{\star}\cdot\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\cdot 2C(\mu,r,m)\cdot\frac{\bar{d}}{d^{\star}}=C(\mu,r,m)\,\bar{d}\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}.

(D.11)

Therefore:

\|Z_{i}\|_{\psi_{1}}\leq C(\mu,r,m)\,\bar{d}\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}.

(D.12)

The mechanism is the same as in the general case:
d^{\star}
from the direction cancels with
1/d^{\star}
from incoherence. The simplification is that the whitened score has
\psi_{1}
-norm
O(1)
, so the
C_{A}\sigma
prefactor disappears.

D.2.5
Step 5: Bernstein concentration

Applying Lemma
C.39
with the variance (
D.9
) and tail bound (
D.12
):

Theorem D.1
(Bound on
R_{\mathrm{emp}}^{\tilde{H}}
).

Under Assumptions
C.3
and the subspace estimation guarantees, with probability at least
1-\delta
(conditional on
\mathcal{D}_{1}
):

\boxed{|R_{\mathrm{emp}}^{\tilde{H}}|\leq C(\mu,r,m)\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\left[\sqrt{\bar{d}}\;\rho\;\sqrt{\frac{\log(2/\delta)}{n}}\;+\;\bar{d}\;\frac{\log(2/\delta)}{n}\right].}

(D.13)

Proof of Theorem
D.1
.

The variance term gives

\sqrt{\frac{\mathrm{Var}(Z_{i})\log(2/\delta)}{n}}\leq C(\mu,r,m)\,\sqrt{\bar{d}}\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\,\rho\,\sqrt{\frac{\log(2/\delta)}{n}}.

The sub-exponential term gives

\frac{\|Z_{i}\|_{\psi_{1}}\log(2/\delta)}{n}\leq C(\mu,r,m)\,\bar{d}\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\,\frac{\log(2/\delta)}{n}.

Combining yields (
D.13
).

\square

D.3
Bounding
R_{\mathrm{emp}}^{\tilde{\eta}}
(score-perturbation empirical process)

Define

R_{\mathrm{emp}}^{\tilde{\eta}}:=(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mathbb{P}^{\star})\big[(\tilde{s}(Y,\hat{\eta})-\tilde{s}(Y,\eta^{\star}))\,\left\langle H^{\star}_{\rm ws},X\right\rangle\big].

Condition on the first-stage output so that
\Delta:=\hat{T}-T^{\star}
and
H^{\star}_{\rm ws}
are fixed.

D.3.1
Taylor expansion of the whitened score

For each
i
, there exists
\bar{\eta}_{i}
between
\hat{\eta}_{i}
and
\eta^{\star}_{i}
such that

\tilde{s}(Y_{i},\hat{\eta}_{i})-\tilde{s}(Y_{i},\eta^{\star}_{i})=\partial_{\eta}\tilde{s}(Y_{i},\eta^{\star}_{i})\left\langle\Delta,X_{i}\right\rangle+\tfrac{1}{2}\partial^{2}_{\eta}\tilde{s}(Y_{i},\bar{\eta}_{i})\left\langle\Delta,X_{i}\right\rangle^{2}.

Hence
R_{\mathrm{emp}}^{\tilde{\eta}}=R_{\tilde{\eta},1}^{\mathrm{emp}}+R_{\tilde{\eta},2}^{\mathrm{emp}}
, where

R_{\tilde{\eta},1}^{\mathrm{emp}}:=(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mathbb{P}^{\star})\big[\partial_{\eta}\tilde{s}(Y,\eta^{\star})\left\langle\Delta,X\right\rangle\left\langle H^{\star}_{\rm ws},X\right\rangle\big],\quad R_{\tilde{\eta},2}^{\mathrm{emp}}:=\tfrac{1}{2}(\mathbb{P}_{n}-\mathbb{P}^{\star})\big[\partial^{2}_{\eta}\tilde{s}(Y,\bar{\eta})\left\langle\Delta,X\right\rangle^{2}\left\langle H^{\star}_{\rm ws},X\right\rangle\big].

D.3.2
Bounds on
\left\lVert H^{\star}_{\rm ws}\right\rVert_{\infty}
and
\left\lVert H^{\star}_{\rm ws}\right\rVert_{F}/\sqrt{d^{\star}}

Since
H^{\star}_{\rm ws}=d^{\star}\,P_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma
, by Lemma
C.33
:

\ell_{\infty}
bound.

Using Lemma
C.37
(
\left\lVert P_{\mathbb{T}}E_{\omega}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq C(\mu,r,m)\,\bar{d}/d^{\star}
):

\left\lVert H^{\star}_{\rm ws}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq d^{\star}\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\cdot\max_{\omega}\left\lVert P_{\mathbb{T}}E_{\omega}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq C(\mu,r,m)\,\bar{d}\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}.

(D.14)

Compared to (
C.29
), the factor
C_{A}\sigma^{2}
is replaced by
1
(since
d^{\star}
from the direction cancels with
1/d^{\star}
from incoherence directly, without passing through
\|A^{-1}\|_{\infty\to\infty}
).

Frobenius bound.

Since
\left\lVert P_{\mathbb{T}}E_{\omega}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}=(P_{\mathbb{T}})_{\omega\omega}\leq C(\mu,r,m)\,\bar{d}/d^{\star}
:

\frac{\left\lVert H^{\star}_{\rm ws}\right\rVert_{F}}{\sqrt{d^{\star}}}\leq d^{\star}\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\cdot\frac{\max_{\omega}\left\lVert P_{\mathbb{T}}E_{\omega}\right\rVert_{F}}{\sqrt{d^{\star}}}\leq C(\mu,r,m)\,\sqrt{\bar{d}}\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}.

(D.15)

D.3.3
Abstract Bernstein bound

The same argument as Proposition
C.11
applies with the whitened score derivatives replacing the original ones.
Since
|\partial_{\eta}\tilde{s}|\leq C_{\dot{\tilde{s}}}
and
|\partial^{2}_{\eta}\tilde{s}|\leq C_{\ddot{\tilde{s}}}
(both constants, see (
D.2
)), the factor
1/\sigma^{2}
in (
C.28
) is replaced by a constant.

Corollary D.2
(Explicit bound for
R_{\mathrm{emp}}^{\tilde{\eta}}
).

Under Assumption
C.3
and comparison design, with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\boxed{|R_{\mathrm{emp}}^{\tilde{\eta}}|\;\leq\;C(\mu,r,m)\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\big(\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}+\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}\big)\left[\sqrt{\bar{d}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(4/\delta)}{n}}\;+\;\bar{d}\,\frac{\log(4/\delta)}{n}\right].}

(D.16)

Proof of Corollary
D.2
.

Substituting (
D.14
) and (
D.15
) into the abstract Bernstein bound (same structure as Proposition
C.11
with constant prefactors):
the first-order variance term gives

C_{\dot{\tilde{s}}}\cdot\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}\cdot\frac{\left\lVert H^{\star}_{\rm ws}\right\rVert_{F}}{\sqrt{d^{\star}}}\leq C(\mu,r,m)\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\,\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}\sqrt{\bar{d}};

the first-order tail term gives

C_{\dot{\tilde{s}}}\cdot\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}\cdot\left\lVert H^{\star}_{\rm ws}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq C(\mu,r,m)\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\,\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}\,\bar{d};

the second-order terms are the same with an extra factor of
\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}
.
Factoring
\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}+\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}
yields (
D.16
).

\square

D.4
Bounding
R_{\mathrm{proj}}
(projection leakage)

The projection leakage term is

R_{\mathrm{proj}}:=\left\langle(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Gamma,\Delta\right\rangle.

This term depends only on the functional gradient
\Gamma
and the estimation error
\Delta=\hat{T}-T^{\star}
; it is
independent
of the score function or the direction
H
.
Therefore, the analysis of Section
C.6
applies
verbatim
.
By Proposition
C.13
and the initialization guarantees:

\boxed{|R_{\mathrm{proj}}|\;\leq\;C(\mu,r,m)\;\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\,\rho^{2}.}

(D.17)

This is identical to (
C.38
).

D.5
H-direction bias

The H-direction bias term is

\mathbb{P}^{\star}\big[\tilde{s}(Y,\hat{\eta})\left\langle\hat{H}_{\rm ws}-H^{\star}_{\rm ws},X\right\rangle\big].

D.5.1
Taylor expansion and leading term

Expanding
\tilde{s}(Y,\hat{\eta})
around
\eta^{\star}
(as in Section
C.7
):

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}^{\star}\big[\tilde{s}(Y,\hat{\eta})\left\langle\hat{H}_{\rm ws}-H^{\star}_{\rm ws},X\right\rangle\big]

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\mathbb{P}^{\star}\big[\tilde{s}(Y,\eta^{\star})\left\langle\hat{H}_{\rm ws}-H^{\star}_{\rm ws},X\right\rangle\big]}_{=\,0\text{ (centering)}}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\mathbb{P}^{\star}\big[\partial_{\eta}\tilde{s}(Y,\eta^{\star})\left\langle\Delta,X\right\rangle\left\langle\hat{H}_{\rm ws}-H^{\star}_{\rm ws},X\right\rangle\big]}_{\text{first-order in }\Delta}

\displaystyle\quad+\underbrace{\tfrac{1}{2}\mathbb{P}^{\star}\big[\partial^{2}_{\eta}\tilde{s}(Y,\bar{\eta})\left\langle\Delta,X\right\rangle^{2}\left\langle\hat{H}_{\rm ws}-H^{\star}_{\rm ws},X\right\rangle\big]}_{\text{second-order in }\Delta}.

(D.18)

D.5.2
First-order piece

By property (ii) of the whitened score,
\mathbb{E}^{\star}[\partial_{\eta}\tilde{s}(Y,\eta^{\star})\mid X]=-1
.
Therefore the first-order piece equals
-\left\langle\Delta,(\hat{H}_{\rm ws}-H^{\star}_{\rm ws})/d^{\star}\right\rangle
computed via the isotropic Gram:

\mathbb{P}^{\star}\big[\partial_{\eta}\tilde{s}\,\left\langle\Delta,X\right\rangle\left\langle\hat{H}_{\rm ws}-H^{\star}_{\rm ws},X\right\rangle\big]=-\left\langle\Delta,G_{0}(\hat{H}_{\rm ws}-H^{\star}_{\rm ws})\right\rangle=-\frac{1}{d^{\star}}\left\langle\Delta,\hat{H}_{\rm ws}-H^{\star}_{\rm ws}\right\rangle.

Since
\hat{H}_{\rm ws}-H^{\star}_{\rm ws}=d^{\star}(\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Gamma
:

-\frac{1}{d^{\star}}\left\langle\Delta,d^{\star}(\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Gamma\right\rangle=-\left\langle\Delta,(\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Gamma\right\rangle.

Decomposing
\Delta=P_{\mathbb{T}}\Delta+(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta
:

•

By Cauchy–Schwarz,

|\left\langle P_{\mathbb{T}}\Delta,(\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Gamma\right\rangle|\leq\left\lVert P_{\mathbb{T}}\Delta\right\rVert_{F}\cdot\left\lVert(\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Gamma\right\rVert_{F}.

Using
\left\lVert(\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Gamma\right\rVert_{F}\leq\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\cdot C(\mu,r,m)\sqrt{\bar{d}}\,\rho/\sqrt{d^{\star}}
(from (
D.8
)) and
\left\lVert P_{\mathbb{T}}\Delta\right\rVert_{F}/\sqrt{d^{\star}}\leq\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}
,
this contributes
O(\sqrt{\bar{d}}\,\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}\cdot\rho\cdot\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1})
, which is an
O(\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}\cdot\rho)
cross term retained in the combined bound.

•

By Hölder’s inequality,

|\left\langle(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta,(\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Gamma\right\rangle|\leq\left\lVert(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}\cdot\left\lVert(\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}.

By Lemma
C.15
,
\left\lVert(\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\leq\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\cdot C(\mu,r,m)
, and by Proposition
C.13
,
\left\lVert(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq C(\mu,r,m)\rho^{2}
.
This gives
O(\rho^{2}\cdot\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1})
.

D.5.3
Second-order piece

Since
|\partial^{2}_{\eta}\tilde{s}|\leq C_{\ddot{\tilde{s}}}
(a constant) and
|\left\langle\Delta,X\right\rangle|\leq 2\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}
:

\left|\tfrac{1}{2}\mathbb{P}^{\star}\big[\partial^{2}_{\eta}\tilde{s}\,\left\langle\Delta,X\right\rangle^{2}\left\langle\hat{H}_{\rm ws}-H^{\star}_{\rm ws},X\right\rangle\big]\right|\leq C_{\ddot{\tilde{s}}}\,\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}\,\mathbb{E}^{\star}[|\left\langle\hat{H}_{\rm ws}-H^{\star}_{\rm ws},X\right\rangle|].

By the
\ell_{1}
-average bound (Lemma
C.14
):

\mathbb{E}^{\star}[|\left\langle\hat{H}_{\rm ws}-H^{\star}_{\rm ws},X\right\rangle|]\leq\frac{\left\lVert\hat{H}_{\rm ws}-H^{\star}_{\rm ws}\right\rVert_{1}}{d^{\star}}.

Since
\hat{H}_{\rm ws}-H^{\star}_{\rm ws}=d^{\star}(\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Gamma
and
\left\lVert(\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\leq\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\cdot C(\mu,r,m)
(by Lemma
C.15
):

\frac{\left\lVert\hat{H}_{\rm ws}-H^{\star}_{\rm ws}\right\rVert_{1}}{d^{\star}}=\left\lVert(\widehat{P}_{\mathbb{T}}-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\leq C(\mu,r,m)\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}.

Therefore the second-order piece satisfies:

\boxed{\left|\text{second-order H-bias}\right|\leq C(\mu,r,m)\,\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}.}

(D.19)

The key cancellation is identical to the general case (Eq. (
C.46
)):
d^{\star}
from the direction cancels with
1/d^{\star}
from the
\ell_{1}
-average bound.
The prefactor
1/\sigma^{2}
from
|\ddot{s}|
is absent here because
|\partial^{2}_{\eta}\tilde{s}|
is bounded by a constant.

D.6
First-order cancellation

The combined first-order cancellation term from the bias decomposition is (same as Eq. (
C.47
)):

\left\langle P_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma,\Delta\right\rangle+\mathbb{P}^{\star}\big[\tilde{s}(Y,\hat{\eta})\left\langle H^{\star}_{\rm ws},X\right\rangle\big]-\mathbb{P}^{\star}\big[\tilde{s}(Y,\eta^{\star})\left\langle H^{\star}_{\rm ws},X\right\rangle\big].

(D.20)

D.6.1
Taylor expansion of the population score difference

The second term of (
D.20
) minus the third (which vanishes by centering) gives, by Taylor expansion around
\eta^{\star}
:

\mathbb{P}^{\star}\big[\tilde{s}(Y,\hat{\eta})\left\langle H^{\star}_{\rm ws},X\right\rangle\big]=-\left\langle\Delta,G_{0}(H^{\star}_{\rm ws})\right\rangle+\tilde{R}_{2}(\Delta;H^{\star}_{\rm ws}),

(D.21)

where we used
\mathbb{E}^{\star}[\partial_{\eta}\tilde{s}(Y,\eta^{\star})\mid X]=-1
(property (ii)) to get
G_{0}(H)=(1/d^{\star})H
, and

\tilde{R}_{2}(\Delta;H^{\star}_{\rm ws}):=\tfrac{1}{2}\mathbb{P}^{\star}\big[\partial^{2}_{\eta}\tilde{s}(Y,\bar{\eta})\left\langle\Delta,X\right\rangle^{2}\left\langle H^{\star}_{\rm ws},X\right\rangle\big].

D.6.2
The cancellation mechanism — complete cancellation

Plugging (
D.21
) into (
D.20
):

\left\langle P_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma,\Delta\right\rangle-\left\langle\Delta,G_{0}(H^{\star}_{\rm ws})\right\rangle+\tilde{R}_{2}(\Delta;H^{\star}_{\rm ws}).

Now
G_{0}(H^{\star}_{\rm ws})=(1/d^{\star})\cdot d^{\star}P_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma=P_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma
.
Therefore:

\left\langle P_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma,\Delta\right\rangle-\left\langle\Delta,P_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma\right\rangle=0.

(D.22)

This is the key structural advantage of score whitening: in the general case (Section
C.8
), the first-order term
\left\langle P_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma,\Delta\right\rangle-\left\langle\Delta,G(H^{\star})\right\rangle
leaves a surviving normal-component contribution
-\left\langle(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta,G(H^{\star})\right\rangle
that requires
O(C_{A}\cdot\left\lVert(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty})
to control (Proposition
C.18
).
Under score whitening,
G_{0}(H^{\star}_{\rm ws})=P_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma\in\mathcal{T}
, so

\left\langle(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta,G_{0}(H^{\star}_{\rm ws})\right\rangle=\left\langle(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta,P_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma\right\rangle=0

by orthogonality of
(I-P_{\mathbb{T}})\Delta\in\mathcal{T}^{\perp}
and
P_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma\in\mathcal{T}
.
Only the second-order remainder
\tilde{R}_{2}
survives.

D.7
Second-order score remainder

The second-order remainder from (
D.21
) is

\tilde{R}_{2}(\Delta;H^{\star}_{\rm ws})=\tfrac{1}{2}\mathbb{P}^{\star}\big[\partial^{2}_{\eta}\tilde{s}(Y,\bar{\eta})\left\langle\Delta,X\right\rangle^{2}\left\langle H^{\star}_{\rm ws},X\right\rangle\big].

Proposition D.3
(Second-order remainder bound).

Under the comparison design, with
|\partial^{2}_{\eta}\tilde{s}|\leq C_{\ddot{\tilde{s}}}
,

|\tilde{R}_{2}(\Delta;H^{\star}_{\rm ws})|\leq C_{\ddot{\tilde{s}}}\,\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}\,\frac{\left\lVert H^{\star}_{\rm ws}\right\rVert_{1}}{d^{\star}}.

(D.23)

Proof of Proposition
D.3
.

Since
|\left\langle\Delta,X\right\rangle|\leq 2\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}
(comparison atom) and
|\partial^{2}_{\eta}\tilde{s}|\leq C_{\ddot{\tilde{s}}}
:

|\tilde{R}_{2}|\leq C_{\ddot{\tilde{s}}}\,\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}\,\mathbb{E}^{\star}[|\left\langle H^{\star}_{\rm ws},X\right\rangle|].

By the
\ell_{1}
-average bound (Lemma
C.14
),

\mathbb{E}^{\star}[|\left\langle H^{\star}_{\rm ws},X\right\rangle|]\leq\left\lVert H^{\star}_{\rm ws}\right\rVert_{1}/d^{\star}
.

\square

Expanding
\left\lVert H^{\star}_{\rm ws}\right\rVert_{1}/d^{\star}
.

Since
H^{\star}_{\rm ws}=d^{\star}P_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma
:

\frac{\left\lVert H^{\star}_{\rm ws}\right\rVert_{1}}{d^{\star}}=\left\lVert P_{\mathbb{T}}\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\leq\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\cdot\max_{\omega}\left\lVert P_{\mathbb{T}}E_{\omega}\right\rVert_{1}\leq C(\mu,r,m)\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1},

where the last step uses Lemma
C.15
(
\left\lVert P_{\mathbb{T}}E_{\omega}\right\rVert_{1}\leq C_{P}=C(\mu,r,m)
, dimension-free).
Therefore:

\boxed{|\tilde{R}_{2}(\Delta;H^{\star}_{\rm ws})|\leq C(\mu,r,m)\,\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}.}

(D.24)

This matches (
C.53
) in form but without
C_{A}
: the
d^{\star}
from the direction cancels with
1/d^{\star}
from the
\ell_{1}
-average bound, and the whitened score derivative is
O(1)
rather than
O(1/\sigma^{2})
.

D.8
Combined bound

We now collect all remainder terms.

D.8.1
Recap of individual bounds

The total remainder
R_{n}
decomposes as:

R_{n}=\underbrace{R_{\mathrm{emp}}^{\tilde{H}}}_{\text{Sec.~\ref{app:ws-RempH}}}+\underbrace{R_{\mathrm{emp}}^{\tilde{\eta}}}_{\text{Sec.~\ref{app:ws-Remp-eta}}}+\underbrace{R_{\mathrm{proj}}}_{\text{Sec.~\ref{app:ws-Rproj}}}+\underbrace{\text{H-bias}}_{\text{Sec.~\ref{app:ws-H-bias}}}+\underbrace{\tilde{R}_{2}(\Delta;H^{\star}_{\rm ws})}_{\text{Sec.~\ref{app:ws-2nd-order}}}.

(D.25)

The individual bounds are:

(i)

Direction-error empirical process
(Theorem
D.1
):

|R_{\mathrm{emp}}^{\tilde{H}}|\leq C(\mu,r,m)\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\Big[\sqrt{\bar{d}}\,\rho\sqrt{\frac{\log(2/\delta)}{n}}+\bar{d}\,\frac{\log(2/\delta)}{n}\Big].

(ii)

Score-perturbation empirical process
(Corollary
D.2
):

|R_{\mathrm{emp}}^{\tilde{\eta}}|\leq C(\mu,r,m)\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\big(\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}+\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}\big)\Big[\sqrt{\frac{\bar{d}}{n}}+\frac{\bar{d}}{n}\Big]

(up to logarithmic factors).

(iii)

Projection leakage
(Eq. (
D.17
)):

|R_{\mathrm{proj}}|\leq C(\mu,r,m)\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\,\rho^{2}.

(iv)

H-direction bias
(Section
D.5
):

\text{first order}\leq C(\mu,r,m)\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\big(\sqrt{\bar{d}}\,\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}\cdot\rho+\rho^{2}\big),\qquad\text{second order}\leq C(\mu,r,m)\,\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}.

(v)

First-order cancellation and second-order remainder
(Sections
D.6
–
D.7
):
first order
cancels completely
(Eq. (
D.22
)); the second-order remainder satisfies

|\tilde{R}_{2}|\leq C(\mu,r,m)\,\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}.

D.8.2
Combined bound

Theorem D.4
(Combined remainder bound for the score-whitened estimator).

Under Assumption
C.3
and the subspace estimation guarantees (P1)–(P5), with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle|R_{n}|\;\leq\;C(\mu,r,m)\,\left\lVert\Gamma\right\rVert_{1}\bigg[

\displaystyle\sqrt{\bar{d}}\;\rho\sqrt{\frac{\log(2/\delta)}{n}}\;+\;\bar{d}\,\frac{\log(2/\delta)}{n}\;+\;

(D.26)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}\sqrt{\frac{\bar{d}}{n}}\;+\;\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}\,\rho\;+\;\rho^{2}+\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}^{2}\bigg],

where
\rho=\sigma\lambda_{\min}^{-1}\sqrt{d^{\star}\bar{d}\log\bar{d}/n}
.
Under the SNR condition,
\rho\asymp\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}\asymp\sqrt{\bar{d}/n}
(up to logarithmic factors), so all terms are the same order; none is negligible.

Remark D.5
(Simplified bound for pairwise comparisons).

In the pairwise-comparison setting (
\sigma=O(1)
,
\lambda_{\min}\asymp\sqrt{d^{\star}}
), substituting
\rho\asymp\sqrt{\bar{d}\log^{c}\bar{d}/n}
,
\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}\lesssim\sqrt{\bar{d}\log^{c}\bar{d}/n}
, and
\delta=\bar{d}^{-c}
into (
D.26
) gives
