The sufficient condition holds when
n_{3}\geq C\,d\log^{C}d
and
\epsilon_{\Theta}\leq c_{\Theta}\sqrt{d}
. Therefore

\left\lVert\widetilde{a}_{j}-a_{j}^{\star}\right\rVert\leq\frac{2R_{j}}{\lambda_{j}}\leq C\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{3}}}+\frac{\epsilon_{\Theta}}{d}+\frac{\epsilon_{\Theta}^{2}}{d^{3/2}}\right)\log^{C}d.

By Proposition
H.3
,
\epsilon_{\Theta}\leq C(d/\sqrt{n_{2}})\log^{C}d
, so

\frac{\epsilon_{\Theta}}{d}\leq C\,n^{-1/2}\log^{C}d\quad\text{and}\quad\frac{\epsilon_{\Theta}^{2}}{d^{3/2}}\leq C\,\frac{\sqrt{d}}{n}\log^{C}d,

with the latter being lower-order. This gives
\left\lVert\widetilde{A}-A^{\star}\right\rVert_{2\to\infty}\leq C\,n^{-1/2}\log^{C}d
.
∎

H.1.6
Final assembly

Theorem H.7
(Entrywise guarantee for the refined estimator).

Assume the standing assumptions in Assumption
H.1
, and suppose
\left\lVert\widehat{T}^{(0)}-T^{\star}\right\rVert_{F}\leq\Delta_{F}\leq C_{\mathrm{init}}\sqrt{d^{3}/n}
. Let
\widetilde{T}=\overline{\Theta}\widetilde{A}^{\top}
be the three-split refinement estimator. Then, with probability at least
1-d^{-c}
,

\left\lVert\widetilde{T}-T^{\star}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq C\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\,\log^{C}d.

By construction,
\widetilde{T}-T^{\star}=(\overline{\Theta}-\Theta^{\star})(A^{\star})^{\top}+\overline{\Theta}(\widetilde{A}-A^{\star})^{\top}
, so

\left\lVert\widetilde{T}-T^{\star}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\left\lVert\overline{\Theta}-\Theta^{\star}\right\rVert_{2\to\infty}\left\lVert A^{\star}\right\rVert_{2\to\infty}+\left\lVert\widetilde{A}-A^{\star}\right\rVert_{2\to\infty}\left\lVert\overline{\Theta}\right\rVert_{2\to\infty}.

By Proposition
H.3
and Lemma
H.4
,
\left\lVert\overline{\Theta}-\Theta^{\star}\right\rVert_{2\to\infty}\leq C(d/\sqrt{n})\log^{C}d
. By Proposition
H.6
,
\left\lVert\widetilde{A}-A^{\star}\right\rVert_{2\to\infty}\leq C\,n^{-1/2}\log^{C}d
. Using
\left\lVert A^{\star}\right\rVert_{2\to\infty}\leq C_{A}\,d^{-1/2}
and
\left\lVert\overline{\Theta}\right\rVert_{2\to\infty}\leq C_{\Theta}\,d^{1/2}
:

\left\lVert\widetilde{T}-T^{\star}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq C\left(\frac{d}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot d^{-1/2}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\cdot d^{1/2}\right)\log^{C}d=C\sqrt{\frac{d}{n}}\,\log^{C}d.

∎

H.2
Tensor Pairwise Refinement: From Frobenius Initializer to Entrywise Guarantee

Proof sketch and order-of-magnitude calculations.

This section extends the matrix refinement of Appendix
H.1
to order-
m
tensors. The key new idea is to work with
combined row objects

x_{s,i}^{\star}:=C_{(s)}^{\star\top}u_{s,i}^{\star}\in\mathbb{R}^{r^{m-1}}
rather than the factor rows
u_{s,i}^{\star}
directly. This reduces the tensor problem to a sequence of matrix-like logistic regression problems, one per mode.

Constant convention.

Throughout this subsection,
C
and
c
denote positive constants that may depend on the structural parameters
(r,\mu,\kappa,B_{0},\alpha)
but not on
d
or
n
; their values may change from line to line.

The proof rests on four governing scale identities (all for fixed
r,m
):

(i)

Combined-row parameter scale:
c\,d^{(m-1)/2}\leq\|x_{s,i}^{\star}\|\leq C\,d^{(m-1)/2}
(product of core scale
d^{m/2}
and factor-row scale
d^{-1/2}
).

(ii)

Context-feature scale:
\|z_{J_{-s}}^{\star}\|=\prod_{t\neq s}\|u_{t,j_{t}}^{\star}\|\leq C\,d^{-(m-1)/2}
(Kronecker product of
m-1
incoherent rows).

(iii)

Rowwise curvature:
\lambda_{s}\geq c\,N\cdot d^{-(m-1)}\geq c\,n/d^{m}
(from the second-moment identity
d^{-1}\sum zz^{\top}=d^{-(m-1)}I
, since each mode-
s
row sees
N
samples with
c\,n/d\leq N\leq C\,n/d
).

(iv)

Scalar nuisance RMS:
\leq C\sqrt{d\log^{C}d/n}
, matching both the per-entry perturbation from imperfect features and the per-entry offset error.

Stage 1: Row-wise combined-object refinement (one mode at a time).

For each mode
s
in turn, fixing the other factors, we solve a row-wise logistic problem (
H.2
) for each row
i\in[d]
. The noise bound from vector Bernstein is
\|N_{s,i}\|\leq C\sqrt{N\log^{C}d}\cdot d^{-(m-1)/2}
, and the bias (from imperfect features and offsets) matches this scale. The strong convexity from (iii) gives a localization bound:

\|\widehat{x}_{s,i}-x_{s,i}^{\star}\|\;\leq\;\frac{\text{gradient norm}}{\text{curvature}}\;\leq\;C\,\frac{\sqrt{N\log^{C}d}\cdot d^{-(m-1)/2}}{N\cdot d^{-(m-1)}}\;=\;C\,d^{(m-1)/2}\sqrt{\frac{\log^{C}d}{N}}\;=\;C\,d^{m/2}\eta,

where
\eta:=\sqrt{\log d/n}
.

Stage 2: Spectral lift from combined-row accuracy to factor accuracy.

The combined-row matrix
X^{(s)\star}=U_{s}^{\star}C_{(s)}^{\star\top}
has singular values between
c\,d^{m/2}
and
C\,d^{m/2}
. The row-wise error
C\,d^{m/2}\eta
from Stage 1 gives Frobenius error
\leq C\sqrt{d}\cdot d^{m/2}\eta
. Applying Wedin’s perturbation theorem to extract the top-
r
left singular vectors
\widehat{U}_{s}
and dividing by the singular value scale
d^{m/2}
, we obtain

\|\widehat{U}_{s}-U_{s}^{\star}\|_{2\to\infty}\leq C\,\eta.

Stage 3: Core reconstruction and final entrywise bound.

After refining all
m
modes, we reconstruct the core
\widehat{\mathcal{C}}
by projecting
\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{(0)}
onto the refined factors. A telescoping decomposition gives

\displaystyle|\widehat{\mathcal{T}}(J)-\mathcal{T}^{\star}(J)|

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\|\widehat{\mathcal{C}}-\mathcal{C}^{\star}\|_{F}\prod_{s}\|u_{s,j_{s}}\|}_{\text{core error}}+\sum_{s=1}^{m}\underbrace{C\,d^{m/2}\cdot\eta\cdot d^{-(m-1)/2}}_{\text{factor-}s\text{ error}}

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{d}\,\eta.

The final entrywise rate
C\sqrt{d\log d/n}
is
independent of the tensor order
m
; all dimension factors cancel through the scale identities.

This section extends the matrix pairwise refinement of Appendix
H.1
to the general order-
m
tensor case.
Starting from a single Frobenius-accurate tensor initializer, we refine one mode at a time using row-wise logistic estimation, and obtain entrywise accuracy

\left\lVert\widehat{\mathcal{T}}-\mathcal{T}^{\star}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq C\sqrt{\frac{d\log d}{n}}.

The final rate is
independent of the tensor order
m
.

H.2.1
Tensor model and assumptions

Fix integers
m\geq 2
and
r\geq 1
, both treated as constants. The signal tensor is

\mathcal{T}^{\star}=\mathcal{C}^{\star}\times_{1}U_{1}^{\star}\times_{2}U_{2}^{\star}\cdots\times_{m}U_{m}^{\star}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times\cdots\times d},\qquad U_{s}^{\star}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times r},\qquad(U_{s}^{\star})^{\top}U_{s}^{\star}=I_{r}.

For each mode
s
, write
C_{(s)}^{\star}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r^{m-1}}
for the mode-
s
unfolding of the core
\mathcal{C}^{\star}
.

Assumption H.8
(Tensor standing assumptions).

(A1)

Fixed rank/order.

m
and
r
are fixed constants.

(A2)

Incoherence.
For each mode
s
,
\left\lVert U_{s}^{\star}\right\rVert_{2,\infty}\leq\mu/\sqrt{d}
for a constant
\mu
.

(A3)

Signal scale.
For each mode
s
,

c_{\mathrm{sig}}\,d^{m/2}\leq\sigma_{r}(C_{(s)}^{\star})\leq\left\lVert C_{(s)}^{\star}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C_{\mathrm{sig}}\,d^{m/2}
.

(A4)

Bounded logits.

\left\lVert\mathcal{T}^{\star}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq B_{0}
. Hence there exist constants
0<c_{\sigma}\leq C_{\sigma}
and
L_{\sigma}<\infty
(depending on
B_{0}
) such that for all
t\in[-4B_{0},4B_{0}]
,
c_{\sigma}\leq\sigma^{\prime}(t)\leq C_{\sigma}
and
\left\lvert\sigma^{\prime\prime}(t)\right\rvert\leq L_{\sigma}
.

H.2.2
Observation model

We use
m+1
independent data splits:
D_{0}
for initialization and
D_{s}
for refinement of mode
s
,
s=1,\dots,m
.
Define
N:=\lfloor n/(md)\rfloor
. For each mode
s
and each active row
i\in[d]
, split
D_{s}(i)
consists of exactly
N
independent comparisons
(J_{-s,\ell},\,w_{\ell},\,Y_{\ell})
for
\ell=1,\dots,N
, where:

(i)

J_{-s,\ell}=(j_{1,\ell},\dots,j_{s-1,\ell},j_{s+1,\ell},\dots,j_{m,\ell})
is uniform on
[d]^{m-1}
;

(ii)

w_{\ell}
is uniform on
[d]\setminus\{i\}
;

(iii)

conditional on
(J_{-s,\ell},w_{\ell})
,

Y_{\ell}\sim\mathrm{Bernoulli}\!\Big(\sigma\big(\mathcal{T}^{\star}_{j_{1},\dots,j_{s-1},i,j_{s+1},\dots,j_{m}}-\mathcal{T}^{\star}_{j_{1},\dots,j_{s-1},w_{\ell},j_{s+1},\dots,j_{m}}\big)\Big).

Thus each mode-
s
row gets
N
samples with
c\,n/d\leq N\leq C\,n/d
.

H.2.3
Initialization guarantee

We assume the initializer built from
D_{0}
satisfies

\left\lVert\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{(0)}-\mathcal{T}^{\star}\right\rVert_{F}\leq A_{0}\,d^{(m+1)/2}\,\eta,\qquad\eta:=\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{n}}.

(H.1)

H.2.4
Algorithm

The estimation procedure is summarized in Algorithm
4
.

Algorithm 4

Tensor pairwise refinement (one-sweep mode-by-mode)

1.

Data splitting.
Partition the data into
m+1
independent splits:
D_{0}
for initialization and
D_{s}
for mode-
s
refinement,
s=1,\dots,m
.

2.

Initialization (Step 0).
Using
D_{0}
, compute a Frobenius-accurate initializer
\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{(0)}
satisfying (
H.1
). For each mode
s
, extract
\bar{U}_{s}^{[0]}
as the top-
r
left singular vectors of the mode-
s
unfolding
\widehat{T}^{(0)}_{(s)}
, then apply the trim-and-orthonormalize map (Definition
H.10
) to obtain
\widehat{U}_{s}^{[0]}
.

3.

Mode-
s
refinement (for
s=1,\dots,m
).
Given current factors
\widehat{U}_{1}^{[s-1]},\dots,\widehat{U}_{m}^{[s-1]}
, using
D_{s}
, for each active row
i\in[d]
:

a)

Construct the nuisance feature
\widehat{z}^{(s)}_{J_{-s}}:=\bigotimes_{t\neq s}\widehat{u}^{[s-1]}_{t,j_{t}}\in\mathbb{R}^{r^{m-1}}
and the clipped offset
\widehat{o}^{(s)}_{w,J_{-s}}:=\Pi_{[-2B_{0},2B_{0}]}\big(\widehat{T}^{(0)}_{\text{opponent entry}}\big)
.

b)

Solve the constrained logistic problem

\widehat{x}_{s,i}:=\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{x\in\mathcal{B}_{s}}\sum_{\ell=1}^{N}\Big[-Y_{\ell}\big((\widehat{z}_{\ell}^{(s)})^{\top}x-\widehat{o}_{\ell}^{(s)}\big)+\log\big(1+e^{(\widehat{z}_{\ell}^{(s)})^{\top}x-\widehat{o}_{\ell}^{(s)}}\big)\Big],

(H.2)

where
\mathcal{B}_{s}:=\{x\in\mathbb{R}^{r^{m-1}}:\left\lVert x\right\rVert\leq M_{x}d^{(m-1)/2}\}
.

Stack the row estimates into
\widehat{X}^{(s)}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times r^{m-1}}
, extract
\bar{U}_{s}^{[s]}
as the top-
r
left singular vectors of
\widehat{X}^{(s)}
, and apply trim-and-orthonormalize to obtain
\widehat{U}_{s}^{[s]}
. For
t\neq s
, set
\widehat{U}_{t}^{[s]}:=\widehat{U}_{t}^{[s-1]}
.

4.

Core reconstruction and output.
Set
\widehat{U}_{s}:=\widehat{U}_{s}^{[m]}
for all
s
and define

\widehat{\mathcal{C}}:=\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{(0)}\times_{1}\widehat{U}_{1}^{\top}\cdots\times_{m}\widehat{U}_{m}^{\top},\qquad\widehat{\mathcal{T}}:=\widehat{\mathcal{C}}\times_{1}\widehat{U}_{1}\cdots\times_{m}\widehat{U}_{m}.

H.2.5
Basic scale identities

Lemma H.9
(Core / combined-row / feature scales).

Under Assumptions
(A2)
–
(A3)
, for every mode
s
and every row
i
:

\displaystyle c_{\mathrm{sig}}\,d^{m/2}\leq\sigma_{r}(C_{(s)}^{\star})\leq\left\lVert C_{(s)}^{\star}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C_{\mathrm{sig}}\,d^{m/2},\qquad\left\lVert u_{s,i}^{\star}\right\rVert\leq\frac{\mu}{\sqrt{d}}.

(H.3)

Hence the combined row object
x_{s,i}^{\star}:=C_{(s)}^{\star\top}u_{s,i}^{\star}\in\mathbb{R}^{r^{m-1}}
satisfies
\left\lVert x_{s,i}^{\star}\right\rVert\leq C\,d^{(m-1)/2}
.
For any context
J_{-s}
, the feature
z^{\star(s)}_{J_{-s}}:=\bigotimes_{t\neq s}u_{t,j_{t}}^{\star}\in\mathbb{R}^{r^{m-1}}
satisfies
\left\lVert z^{\star(s)}_{J_{-s}}\right\rVert\leq C\,d^{-(m-1)/2}
.
Therefore
\left\lvert\left\langle x_{s,i}^{\star},z^{\star(s)}_{J_{-s}}\right\rangle\right\rvert\leq C
.

The first line is Assumptions
(A3)
and
(A2)
. For the combined row,

\left\lVert x_{s,i}^{\star}\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert C_{(s)}^{\star}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\left\lVert u_{s,i}^{\star}\right\rVert\leq C_{\mathrm{sig}}\,d^{m/2}\cdot\frac{\mu}{\sqrt{d}}=C\,d^{(m-1)/2}.

For the feature,
\left\lVert z^{\star}\right\rVert=\prod_{t\neq s}\left\lVert u_{t,j_{t}}^{\star}\right\rVert\leq(\mu/\sqrt{d})^{m-1}=C\,d^{-(m-1)/2}
. The last bound is Cauchy–Schwarz.
∎

H.2.6
The trim-and-orthonormalize map

Definition H.10
(Trim-and-orthonormalize).

Given
V\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times r}
, first clip row norms:
\mathrm{clip}_{\tau}(V)_{i,:}:=v_{i}\cdot\min\{1,\tau/\left\lVert v_{i}\right\rVert\}
with
\tau=2\mu/\sqrt{d}
. Set
\widetilde{V}:=\mathrm{clip}_{\tau}(V)
,
Q(V):=(\widetilde{V}^{\top}\widetilde{V})^{-1/2}
, and
\mathcal{T}(V):=\widetilde{V}\,Q(V)
, whenever
\widetilde{V}^{\top}\widetilde{V}
is invertible.

Lemma H.11
(Properties of trim-and-orthonormalize).

Let
U^{\star}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times r}
satisfy
(U^{\star})^{\top}U^{\star}=I_{r}
and
\left\lVert U^{\star}\right\rVert_{2,\infty}\leq\mu/\sqrt{d}
. Let
V
satisfy
\left\lVert V-U^{\star}\right\rVert_{F}\leq\varepsilon_{F}
with
\varepsilon_{F}\leq c_{0}
for a sufficiently small constant
c_{0}
.

(i)

\mathcal{T}(V)^{\top}\mathcal{T}(V)=I_{r}
,
\left\lVert\mathcal{T}(V)\right\rVert_{2,\infty}\leq C\mu d^{-1/2}
, and
\left\lVert\mathcal{T}(V)-U^{\star}\right\rVert_{F}\leq C\varepsilon_{F}
.

(ii)

If additionally
\left\lVert V-U^{\star}\right\rVert_{2,\infty}\leq\varepsilon_{\mathrm{row}}
, then

\left\lVert\mathcal{T}(V)-U^{\star}\right\rVert_{2,\infty}\leq C\big(\varepsilon_{\mathrm{row}}+d^{-1/2}\varepsilon_{F}\big).

(H.4)

Since
U^{\star}
has row norms
\leq\mu/\sqrt{d}=\tau/2
, clipping is a projection onto a convex set containing
U^{\star}
, hence non-expansive:
\left\lVert\widetilde{V}-U^{\star}\right\rVert_{F}\leq\varepsilon_{F}
and
\left\lVert\widetilde{V}-U^{\star}\right\rVert_{2,\infty}\leq\varepsilon_{\mathrm{row}}
(when the row-wise bound is given). Writing
\widetilde{V}=U^{\star}+E
with
\left\lVert E\right\rVert_{F}\leq\varepsilon_{F}
, we get

\left\lVert\widetilde{V}^{\top}\widetilde{V}-I\right\rVert_{2}\leq 2\left\lVert E\right\rVert_{F}+\left\lVert E\right\rVert_{F}^{2}\leq 2\varepsilon_{F}+\varepsilon_{F}^{2}.

For
c_{0}
small, this is
\leq 1/2
, so
Q(V)
is well-defined with
\left\lVert Q(V)-I\right\rVert_{2}\leq C\varepsilon_{F}
and
\left\lVert Q(V)\right\rVert_{2}\leq 2
. Part (i) follows: orthonormality is by construction;

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{T}(V)\right\rVert_{2,\infty}\leq\left\lVert\widetilde{V}\right\rVert_{2,\infty}\left\lVert Q(V)\right\rVert_{2}\leq 4\mu/\sqrt{d}

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathcal{T}(V)-U^{\star}\right\rVert_{F}\leq\left\lVert\widetilde{V}-U^{\star}\right\rVert_{F}+\left\lVert\widetilde{V}\right\rVert_{2}\left\lVert Q(V)-I\right\rVert_{2}\leq C\varepsilon_{F}.

Part
(ii)
follows from

\left\lVert\mathcal{T}(V)-U^{\star}\right\rVert_{2,\infty}\leq\left\lVert\widetilde{V}-U^{\star}\right\rVert_{2,\infty}+\left\lVert\widetilde{V}\right\rVert_{2,\infty}\left\lVert Q(V)-I\right\rVert_{2}\leq\varepsilon_{\mathrm{row}}+(2\mu/\sqrt{d})\cdot C\varepsilon_{F}.

∎

H.2.7
Initialization: from tensor Frobenius bound to row-bounded factors

Lemma H.12
(HOSVD subspace error).

For each mode
s
, let
\bar{U}_{s}^{[0]}
be the top-
r
left singular vectors of
\widehat{T}^{(0)}_{(s)}
. Then there exists
R_{s}^{[0]}\in O(r)
such that

\left\lVert\bar{U}_{s}^{[0]}-U_{s}^{\star}R_{s}^{[0]}\right\rVert_{F}\leq C\frac{\left\lVert\widehat{T}_{(s)}^{(0)}-T_{(s)}^{\star}\right\rVert_{F}}{\sigma_{r}(C_{(s)}^{\star})}\leq C\sqrt{d}\,\eta.

(H.5)

Standard Wedin/Davis–Kahan perturbation. Since

\left\lVert\widehat{T}_{(s)}^{(0)}-T_{(s)}^{\star}\right\rVert_{F}=\left\lVert\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{(0)}-\mathcal{T}^{\star}\right\rVert_{F}\leq A_{0}d^{(m+1)/2}\eta,\quad\sigma_{r}(T_{(s)}^{\star})=\sigma_{r}(C_{(s)}^{\star})\geq c_{\mathrm{sig}}\,d^{m/2},

we get
C\cdot d^{(m+1)/2}\eta/d^{m/2}=C\sqrt{d}\,\eta
.
∎

Gauge-reset convention.
After Lemma
H.12
, we may perform a global gauge reset so that
\left\lVert\bar{U}_{s}^{[0]}-U_{s}^{\star}\right\rVert_{F}\leq C\sqrt{d}\,\eta
for every
s
. During the induction, each gauge reset is local to the active mode only, so previously established bounds remain valid.

Corollary H.13
(Row-bounded initial factors).

Assume
n\geq Cd\log d
so that
\sqrt{d}\,\eta\leq c_{0}
. Define
\widehat{U}_{s}^{[0]}:=\mathcal{T}(\bar{U}_{s}^{[0]})
. Then

(\widehat{U}_{s}^{[0]})^{\top}\widehat{U}_{s}^{[0]}=I_{r},\qquad\left\lVert\widehat{U}_{s}^{[0]}\right\rVert_{2,\infty}\leq Cd^{-1/2},\qquad\left\lVert\widehat{U}_{s}^{[0]}-U_{s}^{\star}\right\rVert_{F}\leq C\sqrt{d}\,\eta.

Apply Lemma
H.11
to
V=\bar{U}_{s}^{[0]}
with
\varepsilon_{F}=C\sqrt{d}\,\eta
.
∎

H.2.8
The row-wise combined-object refinement theorem

This is the heart of the proof. We state and prove the result for a generic mode
s
.

Fix
s\in[m]
. Assume we have nuisance factors
\widetilde{U}_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times r}
,
t\neq s
, satisfying

\widetilde{U}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{U}_{t}=I_{r},\qquad\left\lVert\widetilde{U}_{t}\right\rVert_{2,\infty}\leq C_{U}d^{-1/2},\qquad\left\lVert\widetilde{U}_{t}-U_{t}^{\star}\right\rVert_{F}\leq A_{U}\sqrt{d}\,\eta.

(H.6)

Define the true combined row objects

x_{s,i}^{\star}:=C_{(s)}^{\star\top}u_{s,i}^{\star},\qquad z_{J_{-s}}^{\star}:=\bigotimes_{t\neq s}u_{t,j_{t}}^{\star},\qquad\widetilde{z}_{J_{-s}}:=\bigotimes_{t\neq s}\widetilde{u}_{t,j_{t}}.

Theorem H.14
(Generic mode-
s
row-wise refinement).

Under Assumptions
H.8
, the initializer bound (
H.1
), and the nuisance-factor assumptions (
H.6
), there exists a constant
C
such that for every active row
i
,

\left\lVert\widehat{x}_{s,i}-x_{s,i}^{\star}\right\rVert\leq Cd^{m/2}\eta

(H.7)

with probability at least
1-d^{-12}
, provided
n\geq Cd\log d
. Consequently,

\left\lVert\widehat{X}^{(s)}-X^{(s)\star}\right\rVert_{2,\infty}\leq Cd^{m/2}\eta,\qquad\left\lVert\widehat{X}^{(s)}-X^{(s)\star}\right\rVert_{F}\leq Cd^{(m+1)/2}\eta.

(H.8)

The proof proceeds by establishing gradient and curvature bounds for the logistic loss, then combining them through a strong-convexity localization argument.

Score equation at the truth.

Define the negative gradient (score) at
x_{s,i}^{\star}
:

S_{s,i}(x):=-\nabla L_{s,i}(x)=\sum_{\ell=1}^{N}\widetilde{z}_{\ell}\bigl\{Y_{\ell}-\sigma(\widetilde{z}_{\ell}^{\top}x-\widehat{o}_{\ell})\bigr\}.

We decompose
S_{s,i}(x_{s,i}^{\star})=N_{s,i}+B_{s,i}
, where the noise
N_{s,i}:=\sum_{\ell}\widetilde{z}_{\ell}\{Y_{\ell}-\sigma(\theta_{\ell}^{\star})\}
has independent mean-zero summands with
\left\lVert\widetilde{z}_{\ell}\right\rVert\leq Cd^{-(m-1)/2}
, and the bias
B_{s,i}:=\sum_{\ell}\widetilde{z}_{\ell}\{\sigma(\theta_{\ell}^{\star})-\sigma(\widetilde{z}_{\ell}^{\top}x_{s,i}^{\star}-\widehat{o}_{\ell})\}
accounts for perturbation of both the design features and the offsets (with
\theta_{\ell}^{\star}:=z_{\ell}^{\star\top}x_{s,i}^{\star}-o_{\ell}^{\star}
).

Noise bound.

We apply vector Bernstein’s inequality to the summands
X_{\ell}:=\widetilde{z}_{\ell}(Y_{\ell}-\sigma(\theta_{\ell}^{\star}))\in\mathbb{R}^{r^{m-1}}
,
\ell=1,\ldots,N
. These are independent and mean-zero conditional on
D_{0}
. Their parameters are:

•

Bernstein radius:
b=\max_{\ell}\|X_{\ell}\|\leq\|\widetilde{z}_{\ell}\|\cdot|Y_{\ell}-\sigma(\theta_{\ell}^{\star})|\leq C\,d^{-(m-1)/2}\cdot 1=C\,d^{-(m-1)/2}
.

•

Variance proxy: since
\mathbb{E}[(Y_{\ell}-\sigma(\theta_{\ell}^{\star}))^{2}]=\sigma(\theta_{\ell}^{\star})(1-\sigma(\theta_{\ell}^{\star}))\leq 1/4
,

\sigma_{N}^{2}:=\sum_{\ell=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}\|X_{\ell}\|^{2}=\sum_{\ell}\|\widetilde{z}_{\ell}\|^{2}\mathbb{E}[(Y_{\ell}-\sigma(\theta_{\ell}^{\star}))^{2}]\leq\frac{1}{4}\sum_{\ell}\|\widetilde{z}_{\ell}\|^{2}\leq C\,N\,d^{-(m-1)}.

Vector Bernstein states

\lx@paragraphsign\bigl(\|\sum_{\ell}X_{\ell}\|\geq t\bigr)\leq(r^{m-1}+1)\exp\bigl(-t^{2}/(2\sigma_{N}^{2}+2bt/3)\bigr)
.
Setting
t=C\sqrt{N\,d^{-(m-1)}\log d}+C\,d^{-(m-1)/2}\log d
and using
r^{m-1}
is a constant:

\left\lVert N_{s,i}\right\rVert\leq C\sqrt{N\log^{C}d}\,d^{-(m-1)/2}

with probability at least
1-d^{-14}
.

Bias bound.

The bias arises from the scalar nuisance perturbation

q_{\ell}:=x_{s,i}^{\star\top}(\widetilde{z}_{\ell}-z_{\ell}^{\star})-(\widehat{o}_{\ell}-o_{\ell}^{\star}).

We decompose this as
q_{\ell}=q_{Z,\ell}-q_{O,\ell}
where
q_{Z,\ell}:=x_{s,i}^{\star\top}(\widetilde{z}_{\ell}-z_{\ell}^{\star})
is the design mismatch and
q_{O,\ell}:=\widehat{o}_{\ell}-o_{\ell}^{\star}
is the offset mismatch.

Step 1: Lipschitz/Cauchy–Schwarz reduction.

Since
\sigma
is
C_{\sigma}
-Lipschitz on the relevant bounded interval,

\displaystyle|

\displaystyle\sigma(\theta_{\ell}^{\star})-\sigma(\widetilde{z}_{\ell}^{\top}x_{s,i}^{\star}-\widehat{o}_{\ell})|

\displaystyle=|\sigma(z_{\ell}^{\star\top}x_{s,i}^{\star}-o_{\ell}^{\star})-\sigma(\widetilde{z}_{\ell}^{\top}x_{s,i}^{\star}-\widehat{o}_{\ell})|\leq C_{\sigma}|q_{\ell}|.

Therefore, by Cauchy–Schwarz applied to
B_{s,i}=\sum_{\ell}\widetilde{z}_{\ell}\{\sigma(\theta_{\ell}^{\star})-\sigma(\widetilde{z}_{\ell}^{\top}x_{s,i}^{\star}-\widehat{o}_{\ell})\}
:

\|B_{s,i}\|\leq C_{\sigma}\Bigl(\sum_{\ell}\|\widetilde{z}_{\ell}\|^{2}\Bigr)^{1/2}\Bigl(\sum_{\ell}q_{\ell}^{2}\Bigr)^{1/2}.

Step 2: Design mismatch population bound.

Define
\Delta_{t}:=\widetilde{U}_{t}-U_{t}^{\star}
for each
t\neq s
. By multilinearity of the Kronecker product,

\widetilde{z}_{J_{-s}}-z_{J_{-s}}^{\star}=\bigotimes_{t\neq s}\widetilde{u}_{t,j_{t}}-\bigotimes_{t\neq s}u_{t,j_{t}}^{\star}=\sum_{q\neq s}a_{q}(J_{-s})+\text{(higher-order cross terms)},

where each first-order term
a_{q}(J_{-s})
has exactly one factor perturbed:

a_{q}(J_{-s}):=u_{1,j_{1}}^{\star}\otimes\cdots\otimes\Delta_{q,j_{q}}\otimes\cdots\otimes u_{m,j_{m}}^{\star}\qquad\text{(with $\Delta_{q,j_{q}}$ in the $q$-th position, $q\neq s$)}.

For the one-
\Delta
terms, the population second moment satisfies

\frac{1}{d^{m-1}}\sum_{J_{-s}}\|a_{q}(J_{-s})\|^{2}=\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}t\neq s\\
t\neq q\end{subarray}}\frac{\|U_{t}^{\star}\|_{F}^{2}}{d}\cdot\frac{\|\Delta_{q}\|_{F}^{2}}{d}=1^{m-2}\cdot\frac{\|\Delta_{q}\|_{F}^{2}}{d}\leq C\,d^{-(m-2)}\cdot\frac{A_{U}^{2}d\eta^{2}}{d}=C\,d^{-(m-2)}\eta^{2},

where we used
(U_{t}^{\star})^{\top}U_{t}^{\star}=I_{r}
and
\|\Delta_{q}\|_{F}^{2}\leq A_{U}^{2}d\,\eta^{2}
from (
H.6
). Higher-order cross terms (with two or more
\Delta
factors) contribute at most
C\,d^{-(m-2)}\eta^{4}
, which is lower-order. The scalar design perturbation
q_{Z}(J_{-s}):=x_{s,i}^{\star\top}(\widetilde{z}_{J_{-s}}-z_{J_{-s}}^{\star})
therefore satisfies

\frac{1}{d^{m-1}}\sum_{J_{-s}}q_{Z}^{2}\leq\|x_{s,i}^{\star}\|^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{d^{m-1}}\sum_{J_{-s}}\|\widetilde{z}_{J_{-s}}-z_{J_{-s}}^{\star}\|^{2}\leq C\,d^{m-1}\cdot d^{-(m-2)}\eta^{2}=C\,d\,\eta^{2}.

Step 3: Offset mismatch population bound.

The offset
\widehat{o}_{\ell}=\Pi_{[-2B_{0},2B_{0}]}(\widehat{T}^{(0)}_{\text{entry}})
is obtained by clipping the initial estimator to
[-2B_{0},2B_{0}]
. Since
|o_{\ell}^{\star}|=|\mathcal{T}^{\star}_{\text{entry}}|\leq B_{0}\leq 2B_{0}
, the true offset lies in the clipping interval, and clipping to
[-2B_{0},2B_{0}]
is
1
-Lipschitz. Therefore

|q_{O}(w,J_{-s})|=|\widehat{o}-o^{\star}|\leq|\widehat{T}^{(0)}_{\text{entry}}-\mathcal{T}^{\star}_{\text{entry}}|.

The conditional second moment (averaging over the opponent
w
and context
J_{-s}
) is

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(d-1)d^{m-1}}\sum_{w,J_{-s}}q_{O}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{(d-1)d^{m-1}}\sum_{w,J_{-s}}|\widehat{T}^{(0)}_{\text{entry}}-\mathcal{T}^{\star}_{\text{entry}}|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\|\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{(0)}-\mathcal{T}^{\star}\|_{F}^{2}}{(d-1)d^{m-1}}\leq\frac{A_{0}^{2}\,d^{m+1}\eta^{2}}{d^{m}}=C\,d\,\eta^{2}.

Step 4: Sampled RMS concentration via scalar Bernstein.

The total perturbation
q_{\ell}=q_{Z,\ell}-q_{O,\ell}
satisfies (combining Steps 2 and 3):

\mathbb{E}[q_{\ell}^{2}]\leq C\,d\,\eta^{2}.

Moreover,
|q_{\ell}|
is uniformly bounded: the design contribution satisfies
|q_{Z,\ell}|\leq\|x_{s,i}^{\star}\|\cdot\|\widetilde{z}_{\ell}-z_{\ell}^{\star}\|\leq C\,d^{(m-1)/2}\cdot d^{-(m-1)/2}=C
, and the offset contribution satisfies
|q_{O,\ell}|\leq 4B_{0}
. Hence
|q_{\ell}|\leq C
(a constant independent of
d
and
n
).

We apply scalar Bernstein’s inequality to
\sum_{\ell=1}^{N}q_{\ell}^{2}
. Define
W_{\ell}:=q_{\ell}^{2}-\mathbb{E}[q_{\ell}^{2}]
. The summands
W_{\ell}
are independent, mean-zero, with:

•

Bernstein radius:
|W_{\ell}|\leq|q_{\ell}|^{2}+\mathbb{E}[q_{\ell}^{2}]\leq C^{2}+C\,d\,\eta^{2}\leq C
(since
d\,\eta^{2}=d\log d/n\leq C
for
n\geq C\,d\log d
).

•

Variance:
\sum_{\ell}\mathbb{E}[W_{\ell}^{2}]\leq N\cdot\mathbb{E}[q_{\ell}^{4}]\leq N\cdot C^{2}\cdot\mathbb{E}[q_{\ell}^{2}]\leq C\,N\,d\,\eta^{2}
.

Scalar Bernstein gives: for
t>0
,

\lx@paragraphsign\Bigl(\sum_{\ell}q_{\ell}^{2}-N\mathbb{E}[q_{\ell}^{2}]\geq t\Bigr)\leq\exp\!\left(-\frac{t^{2}/2}{C\,N\,d\,\eta^{2}+C\,t/3}\right).

Setting
t=C(\sqrt{N\,d\,\eta^{2}\log d}+\log d)\leq C\log^{C}d
and using

N\,d\,\eta^{2}=N\cdot\frac{d\log d}{n}\leq C\,\frac{n}{d}\cdot\frac{d\log d}{n}=C\log d,

we obtain

\sum_{\ell}q_{\ell}^{2}\leq N\,\mathbb{E}[q_{\ell}^{2}]+C\log^{C}d\leq C\,N\,d\,\eta^{2}+C\log^{C}d\leq C\log^{C}d

with probability at least
1-d^{-14}
.

Step 5: Combine.

From Step 1,
\|B_{s,i}\|\leq C_{\sigma}(\sum_{\ell}\|\widetilde{z}_{\ell}\|^{2})^{1/2}(\sum_{\ell}q_{\ell}^{2})^{1/2}
. Since
\|\widetilde{z}_{\ell}\|\leq C\,d^{-(m-1)/2}
, we have
\sum_{\ell}\|\widetilde{z}_{\ell}\|^{2}\leq C\,N\,d^{-(m-1)}
. Combining with Step 4:

\|B_{s,i}\|\leq C_{\sigma}\sqrt{C\,N\,d^{-(m-1)}}\cdot\sqrt{C\log^{C}d}=C\sqrt{N\log^{C}d}\,d^{-(m-1)/2},

matching the noise bound.

Curvature.

Since
\left\lVert\widetilde{U}_{t}\right\rVert_{2,\infty}\leq C_{U}d^{-1/2}
, the features satisfy
\left\lVert\widetilde{z}_{J_{-s}}\right\rVert\leq Cd^{-(m-1)/2}
, and all predictors
\widetilde{z}_{\ell}^{\top}x-\widehat{o}_{\ell}
lie in a bounded interval for
x\in\mathcal{B}_{s}
, so
\sigma^{\prime}(\widetilde{z}_{\ell}^{\top}x-\widehat{o}_{\ell})\geq c_{\sigma}>0
. The Hessian satisfies

\nabla^{2}L_{s,i}(x)=\sum_{\ell}\sigma^{\prime}(\widetilde{z}_{\ell}^{\top}x-\widehat{o}_{\ell})\,\widetilde{z}_{\ell}\widetilde{z}_{\ell}^{\top}\succeq c_{\sigma}\sum_{\ell}\widetilde{z}_{\ell}\widetilde{z}_{\ell}^{\top}.

The orthonormality
\widetilde{U}_{t}^{\top}\widetilde{U}_{t}=I_{r}
gives the population covariance

\frac{1}{d^{m-1}}\sum_{J_{-s}}\widetilde{z}_{J_{-s}}\widetilde{z}_{J_{-s}}^{\top}=\bigotimes_{t\neq s}\Bigl(\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j_{t}}\widetilde{u}_{t,j_{t}}\widetilde{u}_{t,j_{t}}^{\top}\Bigr)=d^{-(m-1)}I_{r^{m-1}}.

We apply matrix Bernstein to the summands
Z_{\ell}:=\widetilde{z}_{\ell}\widetilde{z}_{\ell}^{\top}-d^{-(m-1)}I_{r^{m-1}}
. These are independent with mean zero. Their parameters are:

•

Bernstein radius:
\|Z_{\ell}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\|\widetilde{z}_{\ell}\|^{2}+d^{-(m-1)}\leq C\,d^{-(m-1)}
.

•

Matrix variance proxy:
\|\sum_{\ell}\mathbb{E}[Z_{\ell}^{2}]\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C\,N\,d^{-2(m-1)}
.

Matrix Bernstein (with ambient dimension
r^{m-1}
, a constant) yields

\Bigl\|\sum_{\ell}\widetilde{z}_{\ell}\widetilde{z}_{\ell}^{\top}-N\,d^{-(m-1)}I\Bigr\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C\bigl(\sqrt{N\,d^{-2(m-1)}\log d}+d^{-(m-1)}\log d\bigr)=o(N\,d^{-(m-1)})

for
N\geq C\log^{C}d
, whence

\nabla^{2}L_{s,i}(x)\succeq c\,N\,d^{-(m-1)}I_{r^{m-1}}\qquad\text{for all }x\in\mathcal{B}_{s}

with probability at least
1-d^{-13}
.

Localization.

By Lemma
H.9
, the true parameter satisfies
\left\lVert x_{s,i}^{\star}\right\rVert\leq C\,d^{(m-1)/2}
, so
x_{s,i}^{\star}\in\mathcal{B}_{s}
for
M_{x}
large enough. The uniform strong convexity gives

L_{s,i}(x)-L_{s,i}(x_{s,i}^{\star})\geq-\left\lVert S_{s,i}(x_{s,i}^{\star})\right\rVert\left\lVert x-x_{s,i}^{\star}\right\rVert+\frac{c\,N}{2d^{m-1}}\left\lVert x-x_{s,i}^{\star}\right\rVert^{2}.

The right-hand side is positive whenever
\left\lVert x-x_{s,i}^{\star}\right\rVert
exceeds
2d^{m-1}\left\lVert S_{s,i}(x_{s,i}^{\star})\right\rVert/(c\,N)
, so the minimizer satisfies

\left\lVert\widehat{x}_{s,i}-x_{s,i}^{\star}\right\rVert\leq\frac{C\,d^{m-1}}{N}\left\lVert S_{s,i}(x_{s,i}^{\star})\right\rVert\leq\frac{C\,d^{m-1}}{N}\cdot\sqrt{N\log^{C}d}\,d^{-(m-1)/2}=C\,d^{(m-1)/2}\sqrt{\frac{\log^{C}d}{N}}.

Since
c\,n/d\leq N\leq C\,n/d
, this equals

C\,d^{(m-1)/2}\sqrt{\frac{d\log^{C}d}{n}}=C\,d^{m/2}\sqrt{\frac{\log^{C}d}{n}}=C\,d^{m/2}\eta\cdot(\log d)^{(C-1)/2},

which is
\leq C\,d^{m/2}\eta
after absorbing the polylogarithmic factor into the constant
C
(recalling
\eta=\sqrt{\log d/n}
). This proves (
H.7
). The matrix bounds (
H.8
) follow by a union bound over rows and
\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert_{F}\leq\sqrt{d}\,\left\lVert\cdot\right\rVert_{2,\infty}
.
∎

H.2.9
Spectral lift: from combined-row accuracy to factor accuracy

Theorem H.15
(Spectral lift).

Let
X^{\star}=UM\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times p}
with
p=r^{m-1}
,
U^{\top}U=I_{r}
,
\left\lVert U\right\rVert_{2,\infty}\leq\mu d^{-1/2}
, and
c_{M}d^{m/2}\leq\sigma_{r}(M)\leq\left\lVert M\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C_{M}d^{m/2}
. Suppose
\widehat{X}
satisfies

\left\lVert\widehat{X}-X^{\star}\right\rVert_{2,\infty}\leq\varepsilon_{\mathrm{row}},\qquad\left\lVert\widehat{X}-X^{\star}\right\rVert_{F}\leq\varepsilon_{F}

with
\varepsilon_{F}\leq c\,d^{m/2}
. Let
\bar{U}
be the top-
r
left singular vectors of
\widehat{X}
. Then there exists
R\in O(r)
such that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bar{U}-UR\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq C\frac{\varepsilon_{F}}{d^{m/2}},

(H.9)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\bar{U}-UR\right\rVert_{2,\infty}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\frac{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{row}}}{d^{m/2}}+\frac{\left\lVert X^{\star}\right\rVert_{2,\infty}}{d^{m}}\varepsilon_{F}+\frac{\left\lVert U\right\rVert_{2,\infty}}{d^{m/2}}\varepsilon_{F}\Big).

(H.10)

In particular, if
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{row}}\leq Ad^{m/2}\eta
,
\varepsilon_{F}\leq Ad^{(m+1)/2}\eta
, and
\left\lVert X^{\star}\right\rVert_{2,\infty}\leq C\,d^{(m-1)/2}
, then

\left\lVert\bar{U}-UR\right\rVert_{F}\leq C\sqrt{d}\,\eta,\qquad\left\lVert\bar{U}-UR\right\rVert_{2,\infty}\leq C\eta.

(H.11)

Frobenius bound.
By Wedin/Davis–Kahan:

\left\lVert\sin\Theta(\bar{U},U)\right\rVert_{F}\leq C\varepsilon_{F}/\sigma_{r}(X^{\star})\leq C\varepsilon_{F}/d^{m/2}.

Define
R:=\mathrm{polar}(U^{\top}\bar{U})
; then
\left\lVert\bar{U}-UR\right\rVert_{F}\leq C\varepsilon_{F}/d^{m/2}
, proving (
H.9
).

Row-wise bound.
Let
\widehat{X}_{r}=\bar{U}\widehat{\Sigma}\widehat{V}^{\top}
be the rank-
r
truncation and
E_{r}:=\widehat{X}_{r}-X^{\star}
. Since
X^{\star}
has rank
r
and
\widehat{X}_{r}
is the best rank-
r
approximation,
\left\lVert E_{r}\right\rVert_{F}\leq 2\varepsilon_{F}
. The decomposition
\widehat{X}_{r}-X^{\star}=(\widehat{X}-X^{\star})P_{\widehat{V}}+X^{\star}(P_{\widehat{V}}-P_{V})
with
\left\lVert P_{\widehat{V}}-P_{V}\right\rVert_{2}\leq C\varepsilon_{F}/d^{m/2}
gives

\left\lVert E_{r}\right\rVert_{2,\infty}\leq\varepsilon_{\mathrm{row}}+C\left\lVert X^{\star}\right\rVert_{2,\infty}\cdot\varepsilon_{F}/d^{m/2}.

Write
\bar{U}=(X^{\star}+E_{r})\widehat{V}\widehat{\Sigma}^{-1}=UB+E_{r}\widehat{V}\widehat{\Sigma}^{-1}
, where
B:=\Sigma V^{\top}\widehat{V}\widehat{\Sigma}^{-1}
. By Weyl,
\left\lVert\widehat{\Sigma}^{-1}\right\rVert_{2}\leq Cd^{-m/2}
, so

\left\lVert E_{r}\widehat{V}\widehat{\Sigma}^{-1}\right\rVert_{2,\infty}\leq Cd^{-m/2}\left\lVert E_{r}\right\rVert_{2,\infty},\qquad\left\lVert U(B-R)\right\rVert_{2,\infty}\leq\left\lVert U\right\rVert_{2,\infty}\cdot C\varepsilon_{F}/d^{m/2},

where
\left\lVert B-R\right\rVert_{2}\leq C\varepsilon_{F}/d^{m/2}
. Combining via
\bar{U}-UR=E_{r}\widehat{V}\widehat{\Sigma}^{-1}+U(B-R)
gives (
H.10
).

The concrete bounds (
H.11
) follow by substituting
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{row}}=Cd^{m/2}\eta
,
\varepsilon_{F}=Cd^{(m+1)/2}\eta
,
\left\lVert X^{\star}\right\rVert_{2,\infty}\leq C\,d^{(m-1)/2}
, and
\left\lVert U\right\rVert_{2,\infty}\leq C\,d^{-1/2}
.
∎

Corollary H.16
(Factor update after spectral lift and trimming).

Assume
n\geq Cd\log d
. Apply Theorem
H.15
to
\widehat{X}^{(s)}
and
X^{(s)\star}=U_{s}^{\star}C_{(s)}^{\star}
, and let
\bar{U}_{s}^{[s]}
be the top-
r
left singular vectors. After a local gauge reset, define
\widehat{U}_{s}^{[s]}:=\mathcal{T}(\bar{U}_{s}^{[s]})
. Then

\displaystyle(\widehat{U}_{s}^{[s]})^{\top}\widehat{U}_{s}^{[s]}=I_{r},\quad\left\lVert\widehat{U}_{s}^{[s]}\right\rVert_{2,\infty}\leq Cd^{-1/2},

(H.12)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\widehat{U}_{s}^{[s]}-U_{s}^{\star}\right\rVert_{F}\leq C\sqrt{d}\,\eta,\quad\left\lVert\widehat{U}_{s}^{[s]}-U_{s}^{\star}\right\rVert_{2,\infty}\leq C\eta.

Theorem
H.14
gives the input bounds. Theorem
H.15
(with
\sigma_{r}(C_{(s)}^{\star})\geq c_{\mathrm{sig}}\,d^{m/2}
and
\left\lVert X^{(s)\star}\right\rVert_{2,\infty}\leq C\,d^{(m-1)/2}
from Lemma
H.9
) gives
\left\lVert\bar{U}_{s}^{[s]}-U_{s}^{\star}\right\rVert_{F}\leq C\sqrt{d}\,\eta
and
\left\lVert\bar{U}_{s}^{[s]}-U_{s}^{\star}\right\rVert_{2,\infty}\leq C\eta
. Apply Lemma
H.11
(ii)
with
\varepsilon_{F}=C\sqrt{d}\,\eta
and
\varepsilon_{\mathrm{row}}=C\eta
.
∎

H.2.10
Full-sweep entrywise guarantee

Theorem H.17
(Full-sweep entrywise guarantee).

Under Assumptions
H.8
and the initialization guarantee (
H.1
), the one-sweep estimator
\widehat{\mathcal{T}}
satisfies

\left\lVert\widehat{\mathcal{T}}-\mathcal{T}^{\star}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq C\sqrt{\frac{d\log d}{n}}

(H.13)

with probability at least
1-d^{-8}
.

The proof proceeds by induction over modes, followed by core reconstruction and a telescoping entrywise bound.

Inductive maintenance of factor bounds.

We claim that for each stage
s=0,1,\dots,m
, after the first
s
modes have been refined, the current factors
\widehat{U}_{t}^{[s]}
satisfy

(\widehat{U}_{t}^{[s]})^{\top}\widehat{U}_{t}^{[s]}=I_{r},\qquad\left\lVert\widehat{U}_{t}^{[s]}\right\rVert_{2,\infty}\leq Cd^{-1/2},\qquad\left\lVert\widehat{U}_{t}^{[s]}-U_{t}^{\star}\right\rVert_{F}\leq C\sqrt{d}\,\eta

(H.14)

for every
t\in[m]
, and moreover if
t\leq s
then the sharper row-wise bound holds:

\left\lVert\widehat{U}_{t}^{[s]}-U_{t}^{\star}\right\rVert_{2,\infty}\leq C\eta.

(H.15)

The base case
s=0
is Corollary
H.13
: the HOSVD plus trim-and-orthonormalize yields all three properties in (
H.14
), and (
H.15
) is vacuous. For the induction step
s-1\to s
, the nuisance factors
\{\widehat{U}_{t}^{[s-1]}:t\neq s\}
satisfy (
H.6
) by the inductive hypothesis, so Theorem
H.14
applies to produce the row-wise combined-object estimates
\widehat{X}^{(s)}
. Corollary
H.16
then yields all bounds in (
H.14
)–(
H.15
) for
\widehat{U}_{s}^{[s]}
. For
t\neq s
,
\widehat{U}_{t}^{[s]}=\widehat{U}_{t}^{[s-1]}
and the gauge reset is local to mode
s
, so all previous bounds are preserved.

Core reconstruction.

Write

\widehat{\mathcal{C}}-\mathcal{C}^{\star}=(\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{(0)}-\mathcal{T}^{\star})\times_{1}\widehat{U}_{1}^{\top}\cdots\times_{m}\widehat{U}_{m}^{\top}+\sum_{s=1}^{m}R_{s},

where
R_{s}
captures the effect of replacing
U_{s}^{\star}
by
\widehat{U}_{s}
in the core projection. The first term has Frobenius norm
\leq\left\lVert\widehat{\mathcal{T}}^{(0)}-\mathcal{T}^{\star}\right\rVert_{F}\leq A_{0}d^{(m+1)/2}\eta
(each multiplication by
\widehat{U}_{s}^{\top}
has operator norm
1
). For each
R_{s}
, passing to the mode-
s
unfolding gives

\left\lVert R_{s}\right\rVert_{F}\leq\left\lVert\widehat{U}_{s}^{\top}U_{s}^{\star}-I_{r}\right\rVert_{F}\cdot\left\lVert C_{(s)}^{\star}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C\sqrt{d}\,\eta\cdot C_{\mathrm{sig}}\,d^{m/2}=Cd^{(m+1)/2}\eta.

Summing over
s
(
m
is fixed):

\left\lVert\widehat{\mathcal{C}}-\mathcal{C}^{\star}\right\rVert_{F}\leq Cd^{(m+1)/2}\eta.

(H.16)

Entrywise bound.

Fix an entry
J=(j_{1},\dots,j_{m})
. The telescoping decomposition gives

\widehat{\mathcal{T}}-\mathcal{T}^{\star}=(\widehat{\mathcal{C}}-\mathcal{C}^{\star})\times_{1}\widehat{U}_{1}\cdots\times_{m}\widehat{U}_{m}+\sum_{s=1}^{m}\mathcal{E}_{s},

where
\mathcal{E}_{s}:=\mathcal{C}^{\star}\times_{1}\widehat{U}_{1}\cdots\times_{s-1}\widehat{U}_{s-1}\times_{s}(\widehat{U}_{s}-U_{s}^{\star})\times_{s+1}U_{s+1}^{\star}\cdots\times_{m}U_{m}^{\star}
.

The core term at entry
J
equals
\left\langle\widehat{\mathcal{C}}-\mathcal{C}^{\star},\widehat{u}_{1,j_{1}}\otimes\cdots\otimes\widehat{u}_{m,j_{m}}\right\rangle
, which is bounded by

\left\lVert\widehat{\mathcal{C}}-\mathcal{C}^{\star}\right\rVert_{F}\prod_{s}\left\lVert\widehat{u}_{s,j_{s}}\right\rVert\leq Cd^{(m+1)/2}\eta\cdot d^{-m/2}=C\sqrt{d}\,\eta.

For each factor term
\mathcal{E}_{s}
:

\left\lvert\mathcal{E}_{s}(J)\right\rvert\leq\left\lVert C_{(s)}^{\star}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\cdot\left\lVert\widehat{u}_{s,j_{s}}-u_{s,j_{s}}^{\star}\right\rVert\cdot\prod_{t<s}\left\lVert\widehat{u}_{t,j_{t}}\right\rVert\cdot\prod_{t>s}\left\lVert u_{t,j_{t}}^{\star}\right\rVert.

By the inductive bounds,
\left\lVert\widehat{u}_{s,j_{s}}-u_{s,j_{s}}^{\star}\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert\widehat{U}_{s}-U_{s}^{\star}\right\rVert_{2,\infty}\leq C\eta
, and all other row norms are
\leq Cd^{-1/2}
. Hence

\left\lvert\mathcal{E}_{s}(J)\right\rvert\leq C_{\mathrm{sig}}\,d^{m/2}\cdot C\eta\cdot(Cd^{-1/2})^{m-1}=C\sqrt{d}\,\eta.

Summing over
s
(
m
is fixed):
\left\lvert\widehat{\mathcal{T}}(J)-\mathcal{T}^{\star}(J)\right\rvert\leq C\sqrt{d}\,\eta=C\sqrt{d\log d/n}
.
∎

Remark H.18
(Comparison with matrix case (
m=2
)).

For
m=2
: features
z_{j}=a_{j}^{\star}\in\mathbb{R}^{r}
with
\left\lVert z_{j}\right\rVert\leq C\,d^{-1/2}
, combined-row
x_{i}=\theta_{i}^{\star}\in\mathbb{R}^{r}
with
\left\lVert x_{i}\right\rVert\leq C\,d^{1/2}
. Core unfolding
C_{(1)}^{\star}\in\mathbb{R}^{r\times r}
with
c\,d\leq\sigma_{r}(C_{(1)}^{\star})\leq C\,d
. All of these are special cases of the general formulas with
m=2
. The general proof reduces to Appendix
H.1
when
m=2
and
r^{m-1}=r
.

H.3
Convex Initialization via Nuclear-Norm Penalization

In the matrix case, we provide a convex initialization that achieves the Frobenius-norm rate needed by the refinement procedure. The proof follows the RSC/decomposable-regularization framework of
Negahban and
Wainwright 2012
, adapted to the pairwise comparison setting. The three main modifications are: (i) the entry-sampling operator is replaced by the pairwise difference operator, (ii) the quadratic loss is replaced by the logistic likelihood, and (iii) the centered gauge
\mathbf{1}^{\top}M=0
removes the per-column null directions inherent to pairwise comparisons.

H.3.1
Formulation

Recall that each observation matrix takes the form
X_{t}=(e_{u_{t}}-e_{v_{t}})e_{j_{t}}^{\top}
, and the negative log-likelihood of a candidate matrix
M
is

\mathcal{L}_{n}(M):=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{t=1}^{n}\bigl[\log(1+e^{\left\langle X_{t},M\right\rangle})-y_{t}\left\langle X_{t},M\right\rangle\bigr].

The convex initialization procedure is summarized in Algorithm
5
.

Algorithm 5

Convex initialization via nuclear-norm penalized logistic regression

1.

Input.
Pairwise comparison data
\{(X_{t},y_{t})\}_{t=1}^{n}
; regularization parameter
\lambda>0
; entry bound
\alpha_{0}\geq\alpha
; target rank
r
.

2.

Convex program.
Solve the nuclear-norm penalized logistic regression:

\widehat{M}\in\mathop{\mathrm{argmin}}_{M\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}}\bigl\{\mathcal{L}_{n}(M)+\lambda\left\lVert M\right\rVert_{\star}\bigr\}\quad\text{subject to}\quad\left\lVert M\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\alpha_{0},\quad\mathbf{1}^{\top}M=0.

(H.17)

3.

Rank-
r
projection.
Compute the rank-
r
SVD truncation
\widehat{T}^{(0)}:=\mathrm{SVD}_{r}(\widehat{M})
. Clip entrywise:
\widehat{T}^{(0)}_{ij}\leftarrow\Pi_{[-\alpha_{0},\alpha_{0}]}(\widehat{T}^{(0)}_{ij})
.

4.

Output.
Return
\widehat{T}^{(0)}
as the Frobenius-accurate initializer for Algorithm
3
.

Here
\left\lVert M\right\rVert_{\star}
is the nuclear norm and
\alpha_{0}\geq\alpha
so that the true matrix
M^{\star}
is feasible. The entrywise constraint ensures that all logistic curvatures remain bounded below, and the centered gauge enforces identifiability.

H.3.2
Population geometry of the pairwise operator

The proof relies on one genuinely pairwise-specific algebraic identity.

Lemma H.19
(Population pairwise quadratic form).

Let
X=(e_{u}-e_{v})e_{j}^{\top}
with
j\sim\mathrm{Unif}([d])
and
\{u,v\}
uniform over unordered row pairs. For every
\Delta\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
with
\mathbf{1}^{\top}\Delta=0
,

\mathbb{E}\bigl[\left\langle X,\Delta\right\rangle^{2}\bigr]=\frac{2}{d(d-1)}\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{F}^{2}.

(H.18)

Fix column
j
. Then
\left\langle X,\Delta\right\rangle=\Delta_{uj}-\Delta_{vj}
. For any zero-sum vector
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\sum_{u<v}(z_{u}-z_{v})^{2}=d\sum_{u=1}^{d}z_{u}^{2}.

Since the
j
th column of
\Delta
has zero sum, averaging over all
\binom{d}{2}
pairs gives

\mathbb{E}\bigl[(\Delta_{uj}-\Delta_{vj})^{2}\mid j\bigr]=\frac{2}{d-1}\sum_{u=1}^{d}\Delta_{uj}^{2}.

A further average over
j\sim\mathrm{Unif}([d])
yields (
H.18
).
∎

This is the pairwise analogue of the population norm-equivalence behind matrix completion; the centered gauge
\mathbf{1}^{\top}\Delta=0
is essential, since
\left\langle X,\Delta\right\rangle=0
identically for
\Delta=\mathbf{1}c^{\top}
.

H.3.3
From quadratic RSC to logistic RSC

The logistic loss is not quadratic, but its Bregman divergence inherits the quadratic curvature on the feasible set.

Lemma H.20
(Logistic curvature reduction).

Assume
\left\lVert M^{\star}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\alpha
and restrict to perturbations
\Delta
with
\left\lVert M^{\star}+\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq\alpha_{0}
. Then

\delta\mathcal{L}_{n}(M^{\star};\Delta):=\mathcal{L}_{n}(M^{\star}+\Delta)-\mathcal{L}_{n}(M^{\star})-\left\langle\nabla\mathcal{L}_{n}(M^{\star}),\Delta\right\rangle\geq\frac{c_{\alpha_{0}}}{2n}\sum_{t=1}^{n}\left\langle X_{t},\Delta\right\rangle^{2},

(H.19)

where
c_{\alpha_{0}}:=\inf_{|x|\leq 2\alpha_{0}}\sigma^{\prime}(x)>0
.

By Taylor’s theorem, for each
t
there exists an intermediate point
\xi_{t}
such that

\delta\mathcal{L}_{n}(M^{\star};\Delta)=\frac{1}{2n}\sum_{t=1}^{n}\sigma^{\prime}(\xi_{t})\left\langle X_{t},\Delta\right\rangle^{2}.

On the feasible set,
|\left\langle X_{t},M^{\star}\right\rangle|\leq 2\alpha
and
|\left\langle X_{t},M^{\star}+\Delta\right\rangle|\leq 2\alpha_{0}
, so every
\xi_{t}
lies in
[-2\alpha_{0},2\alpha_{0}]
and
\sigma^{\prime}(\xi_{t})\geq c_{\alpha_{0}}
.
∎

Combining Lemma
H.20
with the quadratic RSC (Theorem
H.21
below) immediately gives logistic RSC: on the appropriate restricted set,

\delta\mathcal{L}_{n}(M^{\star};\Delta)\geq\frac{c\,c_{\alpha_{0}}}{d^{2}}\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{F}^{2}.

(H.20)

H.3.4
Quadratic restricted strong convexity

Following
Negahban and
Wainwright 2012
, define the restricted set of centered perturbations

\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pw}}(n;c_{0}):=\Bigl\{\Delta\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}:\mathbf{1}^{\top}\Delta=0,\quad\alpha_{\mathrm{sp}}(\Delta)\,\beta_{\mathrm{ra}}(\Delta)\leq\frac{1}{c_{0}}\sqrt{\frac{n}{d\log d}}\Bigr\},

where
\alpha_{\mathrm{sp}}(\Delta):=d\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\infty}/\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{F}
and
\beta_{\mathrm{ra}}(\Delta):=\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{\star}/\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{F}
.

Theorem H.21
(Pairwise quadratic RSC).

There exist universal constants
c_{0},c>0
such that whenever
n\geq Cd\log d
, with probability at least
1-d^{-c}
,

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{t=1}^{n}\left\langle X_{t},\Delta\right\rangle^{2}\geq\frac{c}{d^{2}}\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{F}^{2}\qquad\text{for all }\Delta\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pw}}(n;c_{0}).

(H.21)

The proof adapts the argument for Theorem 1 of
Negahban and
Wainwright 2012
to the pairwise difference operator.

H.3.4.1
Peeling reduction.

Define
F_{\Delta}:=(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{t}\left\langle X_{t},\Delta\right\rangle^{2})^{1/2}
and
\mu_{\Delta}:=\sqrt{2/(d(d-1))}\,\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{F}
, so that
\mathbb{E}[F_{\Delta}^{2}]=\mu_{\Delta}^{2}
by Lemma
H.19
. Since
\mu_{\Delta}\geq\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{F}/d
, it suffices to show
F_{\Delta}\geq\mu_{\Delta}/2
uniformly on the restricted set.
Partition the Frobenius range of the restricted set into dyadic shells
S_{\ell}
with
\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{F}\in[\alpha^{\ell-1}\mu_{0},\,\alpha^{\ell}\mu_{0}]
for
\alpha=7/6
; a union bound over shells reduces the problem to a single-scale event at each level
D
.

H.3.4.2
Discretization.

At scale
D
, take a
(D/8)
-net
\{\Delta_{1},\dots,\Delta_{N_{0}}\}
of the localized set in Frobenius norm. By the Sudakov minoration inequality and nuclear-norm/operator-norm duality, the log-covering number satisfies

\log N_{0}\leq C\frac{\rho(D)^{2}}{(D/8)^{2}}\,d,\qquad\rho(D):=\frac{D^{2}}{c_{0}\sqrt{d\log d/n}}.

The reverse triangle inequality gives

F_{\Delta}\geq F_{\Delta_{k}}-F_{\Sigma}

for the nearest net element
\Delta_{k}
and remainder
\Sigma:=\Delta-\Delta_{k}
with
\left\lVert\Sigma\right\rVert_{F}\leq D/8
.

H.3.4.3
Net lower tail.

For each fixed
\Delta_{k}
, the map
(\left\langle X_{1},\Delta_{k}\right\rangle,\dots,\left\langle X_{n},\Delta_{k}\right\rangle)\mapsto F_{\Delta_{k}}
is
(1/\sqrt{n})
-Lipschitz with bounded coordinates
|\left\langle X_{t},\Delta_{k}\right\rangle|\leq 2/d
. Product-space Lipschitz concentration gives the one-sided lower tail

P\Bigl[F_{\Delta_{k}}<\frac{1}{d}\left\lVert\Delta_{k}\right\rVert_{F}-t-\frac{C}{d\sqrt{n}}\Bigr]\leq 4\exp\Bigl(-\frac{nd^{2}t^{2}}{64}\Bigr).

Setting
t=D/(8d)
and taking the union over net elements: the exponential in
t
absorbs
\log N_{0}
for
c_{0}
large enough.

H.3.4.4
Remainder supremum.

The supremum
\sup_{\left\lVert\Sigma\right\rVert_{F}\leq D/8,\,\left\lVert\Sigma\right\rVert_{\star}\leq 2\rho(D)}F_{\Sigma}
is controlled by symmetrization, the Ledoux–Talagrand contraction inequality (from
x^{2}
to
|x|
), and operator/nuclear-norm duality, yielding

\mathbb{E}\Bigl[\sup_{\Sigma}F_{\Sigma}^{2}\Bigr]\leq\frac{CD^{2}}{d^{2}}\Bigl(\frac{C_{\mathrm{rad}}}{c_{0}}+\frac{1}{16}\Bigr),

where
C_{\mathrm{rad}}
bounds the Rademacher sum
\mathbb{E}[\left\lVert\frac{1}{n}\sum_{t}\varepsilon_{t}X_{t}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}]\leq C_{\mathrm{rad}}\sqrt{\log d/(nd)}
via the matrix Bernstein inequality. Choosing
c_{0}
large enough makes the expectation
\leq D^{2}/(8d^{2})
, and the product-space concentration upgrades this to a high-probability bound
\sup_{\Sigma}F_{\Sigma}\leq D/(2d)
.

Combining the net lower tail and remainder supremum verifies the hypothesis of the peeling lemma, closing the induction over shells.
∎

H.3.5
Gradient bound and main theorem

Lemma H.22
(Gradient operator-norm bound).

With probability at least
1-d^{-c}
,

\left\lVert\nabla\mathcal{L}_{n}(M^{\star})\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{nd}}.

(H.22)

The gradient at the truth is

\nabla\mathcal{L}_{n}(M^{\star})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{t=1}^{n}\bigl(\sigma(\left\langle X_{t},M^{\star}\right\rangle)-y_{t}\bigr)X_{t}.

Each summand is zero-mean (by the model), with operator norm at most
\sqrt{2}
.
For the right variance:
\mathbb{E}[X_{t}^{\top}X_{t}]=(2/d)I_{d}
, and

\mathbb{E}\bigl[(\sigma(\left\langle X_{t},M^{\star}\right\rangle)-y_{t})^{2}\,X_{t}^{\top}X_{t}\bigr]\preceq\tfrac{1}{4}\mathbb{E}[X_{t}^{\top}X_{t}]=\frac{1}{2d}I_{d},

since
\sigma^{\prime}(x)\leq 1/4
everywhere. The left variance is bounded similarly by
O(1/d)
.
The matrix Bernstein inequality with variance proxy
O(n/d)
and range
\sqrt{2}
yields (
H.22
).
∎

We are now ready to prove the main initialization theorem.

Theorem H.23
(Convex initialization for pairwise matrix).

Under the model assumptions with
n\geq C\,d\log^{C}d
and
\lambda=C_{\lambda}\sqrt{d\log d/n}
for an appropriate constant
C_{\lambda}
, the nuclear-norm penalized estimator (
H.17
) satisfies

\left\lVert\widehat{M}-M^{\star}\right\rVert_{F}\leq C\sqrt{\frac{d^{3}\log^{C}d}{n}}

with probability at least
1-d^{-c}
.

Set
\widehat{\Delta}:=\widehat{M}-M^{\star}
and choose
\lambda=2C\sqrt{\log d/(nd)}
per Lemma
H.22
, so that
\lambda\geq 2\left\lVert\nabla\mathcal{L}_{n}(M^{\star})\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}
with high probability.

H.3.5.1
Basic inequality.

Since
M^{\star}
is feasible, the optimality of
\widehat{M}
gives

\delta\mathcal{L}_{n}(M^{\star};\widehat{\Delta})\leq-\left\langle\nabla\mathcal{L}_{n}(M^{\star}),\widehat{\Delta}\right\rangle+\lambda\bigl(\left\lVert M^{\star}\right\rVert_{\star}-\left\lVert M^{\star}+\widehat{\Delta}\right\rVert_{\star}\bigr).

By the operator/nuclear-norm duality
|\left\langle\nabla\mathcal{L}_{n}(M^{\star}),\widehat{\Delta}\right\rangle|\leq\frac{\lambda}{2}\left\lVert\widehat{\Delta}\right\rVert_{\star}
, and by the standard decomposability of the nuclear norm,

\left\lVert M^{\star}\right\rVert_{\star}-\left\lVert M^{\star}+\widehat{\Delta}\right\rVert_{\star}\leq\left\lVert\widehat{\Delta}_{T}\right\rVert_{\star}-\left\lVert\widehat{\Delta}_{T^{\perp}}\right\rVert_{\star},

where
T
is the tangent space of rank-
r
matrices at
M^{\star}
. Combining,

\delta\mathcal{L}_{n}(M^{\star};\widehat{\Delta})\leq\tfrac{3\lambda}{2}\left\lVert\widehat{\Delta}_{T}\right\rVert_{\star}-\tfrac{\lambda}{2}\left\lVert\widehat{\Delta}_{T^{\perp}}\right\rVert_{\star}.

Since
\delta\mathcal{L}_{n}\geq 0
, this yields the cone condition
\left\lVert\widehat{\Delta}_{T^{\perp}}\right\rVert_{\star}\leq 3\left\lVert\widehat{\Delta}_{T}\right\rVert_{\star}
and, using
\mathrm{rank}(\widehat{\Delta}_{T})\leq 2r
,

\left\lVert\widehat{\Delta}\right\rVert_{\star}\leq 4\sqrt{2r}\,\left\lVert\widehat{\Delta}\right\rVert_{F}.

H.3.5.2
Frobenius bound.

On the event of Theorem
H.21
, if
\widehat{\Delta}\in\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pw}}(n;c_{0})
, the logistic RSC (
H.20
) gives
\delta\mathcal{L}_{n}(M^{\star};\widehat{\Delta})\geq\kappa_{\mathrm{pw}}\left\lVert\widehat{\Delta}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}
with
\kappa_{\mathrm{pw}}\geq c\,c_{\alpha_{0}}/d^{2}
. The basic inequality then yields

\kappa_{\mathrm{pw}}\left\lVert\widehat{\Delta}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}\leq\tfrac{3\lambda}{2}\sqrt{2r}\,\left\lVert\widehat{\Delta}\right\rVert_{F},

so
\left\lVert\widehat{\Delta}\right\rVert_{F}\leq 3\lambda\sqrt{2r}/(2\kappa_{\mathrm{pw}})\leq C\,\lambda\sqrt{r}\,d^{2}=C\sqrt{d^{3}r\log d/n}
.

If instead
\widehat{\Delta}\notin\mathcal{C}_{\mathrm{pw}}(n;c_{0})
, the cone condition and the entrywise feasibility constraint
\left\lVert\widehat{\Delta}\right\rVert_{\infty}\leq 2\alpha_{0}
combine to give
\left\lVert\widehat{\Delta}\right\rVert_{F}\leq C\,\alpha_{0}\sqrt{r}\,d\sqrt{\log d/n}
, which is bounded by the same rate for
n\geq C\,d\log d
.
∎

Remark H.24
.

The rate
\left\lVert\widehat{M}-M^{\star}\right\rVert_{F}\leq C\sqrt{d^{3}r\log d/n}
differs from the Negahban–Wainwright matrix-completion rate
C\sqrt{dr\log d/n}
by a factor of
d
. This reflects the
d^{-2}
gap between the pairwise population identity (
H.18
) and the matrix-completion identity
\mathbb{E}[\left\langle X,\Delta\right\rangle^{2}]=\left\lVert\Delta\right\rVert_{F}^{2}
: each pairwise comparison carries
O(1/d^{2})
of the information per entry, making the
d
-factor overhead unavoidable.
